Proof of Lemma
2.24

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\backslash a=(E^{\prime},\,\leq^{\prime},\,\#^{\prime})

We need to show that
\leq^{\prime}
is a partial order and
\#^{\prime}
is symmetric and irreflexive. Since

\leq^{\prime}
and
\#^{\prime}
respectively equal
\leq
and
\#
for the events in
E^{\prime}
, it follows directly
that
\leq^{\prime}
is a partial order and
\#^{\prime}
is symmetric and irreflexive.

We need to show that
\forall e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E
:

1.

\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq e}
is finite

Since
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq e}
is finite, then so it is

\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq^{\prime}e}=\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq e}\backslash l
.

2.

e\#e^{\prime}\leq e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow e\#e^{\prime\prime}

Let
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E^{\prime}
. Then
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\neq a
and
\neg(e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\#a)
. By
Definition
2.23
,
e\#^{\prime}e^{\prime}
entails
e\#e^{\prime}
and
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
and

e^{\prime}\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
entails
e^{\prime}\leq e^{\prime\prime}
and
e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E
. Since
\mathrm{E}
is an event structure,
we have
e\#e^{\prime}\leq e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow e\#e^{\prime\prime}
. Thus

e\#^{\prime}e^{\prime}\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow e\#^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
and
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.25

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}=(E^{\prime}_{1},\,\leq^{\prime}_{1},\,\#^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}=(E^{\prime}_{2},\,\leq^{\prime}_{2},\,\#^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}=(E^{\prime},\,\leq^{\prime},\,\#^{\prime})

such that
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
.

1.

We start by defining
f:E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
such that

f(e)=\begin{cases}f_{1}(e)&\text{ if }e\in E_{1}\\
(f_{2}(e),m_{1}(x))&\text{ if }e\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})\end{cases}

where
m_{1}(x)
is defined as follows:
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
,

m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
such that
f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
. If multiple are possible,

m_{1}(x)
is arbitrarily chosen among them.

Now we show that
f
is injective,
i.e.
if
a\neq b
then
f(a)\neq f(b)
.
We have two cases:

•

if
a,b\in E_{1}
we are done

•

if
a,b\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})

Let
a=(e,x)
and
b=(e,y)
.
For
(e,x)\neq(e^{\prime},y)
we have two cases:

–

e\neq e^{\prime}

We then have

\displaystyle f(e,x)\neq f(e^{\prime},y)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(f_{2}(e),m_{1}(x))\neq(f_{2}(e^{\prime}),m_{1}(y))

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow f_{2}(e)\neq f_{2}(e^{\prime})\text{ or }m_{1}(x)\neq m_{1}(y)

Since
f_{2}
is injective we are done.

–

e=e^{\prime}
and
x\neq y

We then have

\displaystyle f(e,x)\neq f(e^{\prime},y)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(f_{2}(e),m_{1}(x))\neq(f_{2}(e^{\prime}),m_{1}(y))

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow f_{2}(e)\neq f_{2}(e^{\prime})\text{ or }m_{1}(x)\neq m_{1}(y)

Since we know that
f_{2}
is an injective function, then we should have
f_{2}(e)=f_{2}(e^{\prime})
. Otherwise we enter in a contradiction. Hence it lacks to see if

m_{1}(x)\neq m_{1}(y)
. From definition of
m_{1}(x)
and
m_{1}(y)
we have, respectively,

f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
and
f_{2}[y]\subseteq m_{2}(y)
. Thus

f_{1}[x],f_{1}[y]\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
. Since
f_{1}
is injective and
x\neq y
then it
exists
e\in x
and
e^{\prime}\in y
, with
e\neq e^{\prime}
, such that
f_{1}(e)\neq f_{1}(e^{\prime})
. Thus

f_{1}[x]\neq f_{1}[y]
, and consequently
m_{1}(x)\neq m_{1}(y)
.

Hence
f
is injective.

2.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)

We have two cases:

•

a\in E_{1}

Then
\pi(f(a))=\pi(f_{1}(a))=\pi(a)
since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
.

•

(a,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})

Then
\pi(f(a,x))=\pi(f_{2}(a),m_{1}(x))=\pi(f_{2}(a))=\pi(a)
since

\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
.

3.

a\leq b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)

\Rightarrow

Assume
a\leq b
.

By Definition
2.14
we have:

(a)

a,b\in E_{1}
.

Hence
a\leq_{1}b
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
, then
f_{1}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
.
By Definition
2.14
,
f_{1}(a)\leq^{\prime}f_{1}(b)
.

(b)

(a,x),(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})

Hence
(a,x)\leq(b,x)
such that
a\leq_{2}b
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}

then
f_{2}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
. Now let
m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
such that

f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
. Then
(f_{2}(a),m_{1}(x))\leq^{\prime}(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))
. By
Definition
2.14
,
f(a,x)\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

(c)

a\in E_{1}
and
(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
such that
a\in x

Hence
a\leq(b,x)
. From
(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
we have
b\in E_{2}
and

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and

\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
, then
f_{1}(a)\in E^{\prime}_{1}
and
f_{2}(a)\in E^{\prime}_{2}
. Now let

m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
such that
f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
. Since
a\in x
then

f_{1}(a)\in f_{1}[x]
. Thus
f_{1}(a)\in m_{1}(x)
. Hence

(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))=f(b,x)\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
. By
Definition
2.14
,
f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)

(a)

f(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f(b)

We have
f(a)=f_{1}(a),f(b)=f_{1}(b)\in E^{\prime}_{1}
. Hence
f_{1}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
.
Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
then
a\leq_{1}b
.
By Definition
2.14
,
a\leq b
.

(b)

f(a,x)\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)

We have

f(a,x)=(f_{2}(a),m_{1}(x)),f(b,x)=(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
.
From
m_{1}(x)
we have
f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
such that
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. From
Definition
2.14
we have
f_{2}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
then
a\leq_{2}b
. By Definition
2.14

(a,x)\leq(b,x)
.

(c)

f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)
such that
f(a)\in m_{1}(x)
.

We have
f(a)=f_{1}(a)\in E^{\prime}_{1}
and

f(b,x)=(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
, which gives

f_{2}(b)\in E^{\prime}_{2}
and
m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
. From
m_{1}(x)
we have

f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
such that
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. Since
f_{1}(a)\in f_{1}[x]

then
a\in x
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}

then
a\in E_{1}
and
b\in E_{2}
. We then have
(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
.
By Definition
2.14

a\leq(b,x)
.

4.

a\#b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)

\Rightarrow

Assume
a\#b
.

From Definition
2.14
we have two cases:

–

\exists(e_{1}\leq a,\ e^{\prime}_{1}\leq b)\ .\ e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}

Here we have two subcases:

*

a,b\in E_{1}

Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
we have
f_{1}(a)\#^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
.

*

a\in E_{1}
and
(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})

Then
e_{1}\leq a,e^{\prime}_{1}\leq(b,x)
such that
e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}
. By
Definition
2.14
,
e_{1}\leq_{1}a
and
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq(b,x)
such that

e^{\prime}_{1}\in x
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
we have

f_{1}(e_{1})\leq_{1}f_{1}(a)\Leftrightarrow f(e_{1})\leq f(a)
and
f_{1}(e_{1})\#^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(e^{\prime}_{1})
.
From
(b,c)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
we have
b\in E_{2}
and

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. Now let
m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
such that

f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
. Since
e^{\prime}_{1}\in x
then
f_{1}(e^{\prime}_{1})\in f_{1}[x]
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
then
f_{2}(b)\in E^{\prime}_{2}
. Thus

(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))=f(b,x)\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
. Hence

f(e^{\prime}_{1})\leq f(b,x)
. Thus
f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

–

(a,x)\#(b,x)
such that
a\#_{2}b

From
(a,x),(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
we have
a,b\in E_{2}
and

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. From
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
we have

f_{2}(a),f_{2}(b)\in E^{\prime}_{2}
and
f_{2}(a)\#^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
. Now let

m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
such that
f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
. Then

(f_{2}(a),m_{1}(x))=f(a,x),(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))=f(b,x)\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
.
Thus
f(a,x)\#^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)

From Definition
2.14
we have two cases:

–

f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)
such that

\exists(f(e_{1})\leq f(a),f(e^{\prime}_{1})\leq f(b))\ .\ f_{1}(e_{1})\#^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(e^{\prime}_{1})

Here we have two subcases:

*

f(a),f(b)\in E^{\prime}_{1}
.

We are done since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
.

*

f(a)\in E^{\prime}_{1}
and
f(b,x)\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})

Then
f(e_{1})\leq f(a)\Leftrightarrow f_{1}(e_{1})\leq f_{1}(a)
and

f(e^{\prime}_{1})\leq f(b,x)\Leftrightarrow f_{1}(e^{\prime}_{1})\leq(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))
. By
Definition
2.14
,
f_{1}(e_{1})\leq_{1}f_{1}(a)
and

f_{1}(e^{\prime}_{1})\leq(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))
such that
f_{1}(e^{\prime}_{1})\in m_{1}(x)
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
,
e_{1}\leq_{1}a
and
e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}
. From

(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
we have
f_{2}(b)\in E^{\prime}_{2}
and

m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
then

b\in E_{2}
. Since
f_{1}(e^{\prime}_{1})\in m_{1}(x)
and
f_{1}[x]\subseteq m_{1}(x)
then

e^{\prime}_{1}\in x
. Thus
(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. Hence
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq(b,x)
.
Thus
a\#(b,x)
.

–

f(a,x)\#^{\prime}f(b,x)
such that
f_{2}(a)\#^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)

From

f(a,x)=(f_{2}(a),m_{1}(x)),f(b,x)=(f_{2}(b),m_{1}(x))\in E^{\prime}_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1})

we have that
f_{2}(a),f_{2}(b)\in E_{2}
and
m_{1}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E^{\prime}_{1}})
. From

\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
we have
a,b\in E_{2}
and
a\#_{2}b
. From
m_{1}(x)
we
know that
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. Thus
(a,x)\#(b,x)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.26

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}=(E^{\prime}_{1},\,\leq^{\prime}_{1},\,\#^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}=(E^{\prime}_{2},\,\leq^{\prime}_{2},\,\#^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,\square\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}\,\square\,\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}=(E^{\prime},\,\leq^{\prime},\,\#^{\prime})

such that
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
.

1.

We start by defining a function
f:E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
such that

f(e)=\begin{cases}f_{1}(e)&\text{ if }e\in E_{1}\\
f_{2}(e)&\text{ if }e\in E_{2}\\
\end{cases}

Clearly
f
is injective, since if
e\in E_{1}
then
f(e)=f_{1}(e)
and
f_{1}
is injective and if

e\in E_{2}
then
f(e)=f_{2}(e)
and
f_{2}
is injective.

2.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)

We have two similar cases,
a\in E_{1}
or
a\in E_{2}
. We only show the former.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(f_{1}(a))=\pi(a)
, since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
.

3.

a\leq b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)

\Rightarrow

Assume
a\leq b
.

By Definition
2.18
,
a\leq_{1}b
or
a\leq_{2}b
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
, then

f_{1}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
and
f_{2}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
. By Definition
2.18
we are
done.

\Leftarrow

Assume
f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)
.

By Definition
2.18

f(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f(b)\Leftrightarrow f_{1}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
or

f(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f(b)\Leftrightarrow f_{2}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
, then
a\leq_{1}b
and

a\leq_{2}b
. By Definition
2.18
we are done.

4.

a\#b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)

\Rightarrow

Assume
a\#b
.

We have the following cases:

–

If
a\#_{1}b
or
a\#_{2}b
, then we are done since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and

\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
.

–

If
a\in E_{1}
and
b\in E_{2}
(or vice-versa).

Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
, then

f_{1}(a)\in E^{\prime}_{1}
and
f_{2}(b)\in E^{\prime}_{2}
. By Definition
2.18
,

f_{1}(a)\#^{\prime}f_{2}(b)\Leftrightarrow f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)
.

We have the following cases:

–

If
f(a)\#^{\prime}_{1}f(b)\Leftrightarrow f_{1}(a)\#^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
or

f(a)\#^{\prime}_{2}f(b)\Leftrightarrow f_{2}(a)\#^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
, we are done since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
.

–

If
f(a)=f_{1}(a)\in E^{\prime}_{1}
and
f(b)=f_{2}(b)\in E^{\prime}_{2}
(or vice-versa).

Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
, then

a\in E_{1}
and
b\in E_{2}
. By Definition
2.18
,
a\#b
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.27

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}=(E^{\prime}_{1},\,\leq^{\prime}_{1},\,\#^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}=(E^{\prime}_{2},\,\leq^{\prime}_{2},\,\#^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}=(E^{\prime},\,\leq^{\prime},\,\#^{\prime})

such that
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
.

1.

We start by defining a function
f:E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
such that

f(e)=\begin{cases}f_{1}(e)&\text{ if }e\in E_{1}\\
f_{2}(e)&\text{ if }e\in E_{2}\\
\end{cases}

Clearly
f
is injective, since if
e\in E_{1}
then
f(e)=f_{1}(e)
and
f_{1}
is injective and if

e\in E_{2}
then
f(e)=f_{2}(e)
and
f_{2}
is injective.

2.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)

We have two similar cases,
a\in E_{1}
or
a\in E_{2}
. We only show the former.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(f_{1}(a))=\pi(a)
, since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
.

3.

a\leq b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)

\Rightarrow

Assume
a\leq b
.

By Definition
2.16
,
a\leq_{1}b
or
a\leq_{2}b
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
, then

f_{1}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
and
f_{2}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
. By Definition
2.16
we are
done.

\Leftarrow

Assume
f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)
.

By Definition
2.16

f(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f(b)\Leftrightarrow f_{1}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{1}f_{1}(b)
or

f(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f(b)\Leftrightarrow f_{2}(a)\leq^{\prime}_{2}f_{2}(b)
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2}
, then
a\leq_{1}b
and

a\leq_{2}b
. By Definition
2.16
we are done.

4.

a\#b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)

Similar to the previous case.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.28

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\equiv\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }\mathrm{E}_{2}\equiv\mathrm{E}_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:pes-sub1}}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{1},\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{2}^{\prime}\text{ and }\mathrm{E}_{2}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{op\text{ monotone}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,op\,\mathrm{E}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\prime}\,op\,\mathrm{E}_{2}^{\prime}\text{ and }\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\prime}\,op\,\mathrm{E}_{2}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{1}\,op\,\mathrm{E}_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:pes-sub1}}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,op\,\mathrm{E}_{2}\equiv\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\prime}\,op\,\mathrm{E}_{2}^{\prime}

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.29

Proof.

To prove
(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\equiv(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}}
, we
need to verify that:

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}}

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}}\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{1\,;\,2},\,\leq_{1\,;\,2},\,\#_{1\,;\,2})

\displaystyle(\mathrm{E}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{2})\backslash l=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l=(E_{1}^{l},\,\leq_{1}^{l},\,\#_{1}^{l})

\displaystyle(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E^{\prime},\,\leq^{\prime},\,\#^{\prime})

\displaystyle l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}})

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}}

1.

We start by defining a function
f:E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
such that

f(e)=\begin{cases}e&\text{ if }e\in E_{1}\\
(e,x\backslash l)&\text{ if }e\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})\end{cases}

where
x\backslash l=\{e\mid e\in x\wedge e\neq l\}
.

First we show that
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
. It is clear that

(x\backslash l)\cup\{l\}=x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
. Furthermore, since

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
then
\nexists y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
such that
x\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \ y
.
Now let
y\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
such that

x\backslash l\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \ y\backslash l
. By adding
l
to both configurations we would have

x\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \ y
, which means that
x\not\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
, hence a contradiction. Thus

x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.

Another way of seeing that
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
is by noting
that
x\backslash l
removes the initial event from
x
, which is itself a maximal
configuration.

Now we show that
f
is injective. We have two cases:

–

a,b\in E_{1}

Then we are done since the mapping is the identity.

–

(a,x),(b,y)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})

From
(a,x)\neq(b,y)
we have two cases:

*

a\neq b

Trivially holds because the function is the identity

*

a=b
and
x\neq y

Then

f(a,x)\neq f(b,y)\Leftrightarrow(a,x\backslash l)\neq(b,y\backslash l)\Leftrightarrow a\neq b\text{ or }x\backslash l\neq y\backslash l
.

Clearly it cannot be the case
a\neq b
, otherwise it would contradict our assumption
that
a=b
, and also the fact that the identity function is injective. Hence it can only
be
x\backslash l\neq y\backslash l
. Since
x\neq y
then
\exists e\in x
such that

e\not\in y
. Since
e\neq l
, then we have
x\backslash l\neq y\backslash l
.

2.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)

We have two cases:

–

a\in E_{1}

It follows directly that
\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)
, since
f(a)=a

–

(a,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})

Then
\pi(f(a,x))=\pi(a,x\backslash l)=\pi(a)

3.

a\leq b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)

\Rightarrow

Assume
a\leq b
.

By Definition
2.23
,
a,b\ \in E
(
i.e.

a,b\neq l
and

\neg(a,b\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
), and
a\leq_{1\,;\,2}b
. We have three cases:

(i)

a\leq_{1\,;\,2}b
is of the form
a\leq_{1}b
.

We have
a,b\in E_{1}
. Note that
f(a)=a,f(b)=b\in E^{\prime}
, hence
a,b\neq l
and

\neg(a,b\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
. By Definition
2.23

a\leq_{1}^{l}b
. By
Definition
2.14

a\leq^{\prime}b
.

(ii)

a\leq_{1\,;\,2}b
is of the form
(a,x)\leq_{1\,;\,2}(b,x)
.

It follows directly that
(a,x\backslash l)=f(a,x)\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)=(b,x\backslash l)

since
a,b\in E_{2}
and
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.

(iii)

a\leq_{1\,;\,2}b
is of the form
a\leq_{1\,;\,2}(b,x)
.

We know that
f(a)=a
and
f(b,x)=(b,x\backslash l)
such that

x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
and
a\in x\backslash l
since

a\neq l
and
\neg(a\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
. By Definition
2.14
,

a\leq^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)
.

We have three cases:

(i)

f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)
comes from
f(a)\leq_{1}^{l}f(b)
.

We know that
f(a)=a
and
f(b)=b
. Hence we have
a\leq_{1}^{l}b
and, consequently,

a,b\neq l
and
a,b\#_{1}l
. By Definition
2.23
,
a\leq_{1}b
. By
Definition
2.14
and Definition
2.23
that
a\leq b
.

(ii)

f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)
is of the form
f(a,x)\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

We know that
f(a,x)=(a,x\backslash l)
and
f(b,x)=(b,x\backslash l)
.
Hence we have
(a,x\backslash l)\leq^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)
.
By Definition
2.14
we have
a\leq_{2}b
.
Again by Definition
2.14
, we have
(a,x)\leq_{1\,;\,2}(b,x)
.
Since
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
, we know that none of the events in
x
are in conflict with
l
.
Hence,
\neg((a,x),(b,x)\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
.
By Definition
2.23
, we have
(a,x)\leq(b,x)
.

(iii)

f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b)
is of the form
f(a)\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

We know that
f(a)=a
and
f(b,x)=(b,x\backslash l)
.
Hence we have
a\leq^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)
, such that
a\in x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
,
a\in E_{1}^{l}
, and
b\in E_{2}
.
By Definition
2.23
, we have
a\in E_{1}
, such that
a\neq l
and
\neg(a\#_{1}l)
, and
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
.
By Definition
2.14
, we have
a\leq_{1\,;\,2}(b,x)
, such that
\neg((b,x)\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
, since
x
has no events in conflict with
l
.
By Definition
2.23
, we have
a\leq(b,x)
.

4.

a\#b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)

\Rightarrow

Assume
a\#b
.

By Definition
2.23
it entails
a\#_{1\,;\,2}b
and
a,b\in E
(
i.e.

a,b\neq l
and
\neg(a,b\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
). By Definition
2.14
it
entails
\exists(e_{1}\leq_{1\,;\,2}a,e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1\,;\,2}b)\,.\,e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}
or

a\#_{2}b
. We have three cases:

(i)

a\#_{1}b
.

Then
e_{1}\leq_{1}a,e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1}b
.
We know that
e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}\neq l
since if
e_{1}=l
then
e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1}b\Rightarrow e_{1}\#_{1}b
, which would entail
b\not\in E
, a contradiction.
Furthermore,
\neg(e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}\#_{1}l)
with similar arguments.
We know that
f(e)=e
for
e\in\{e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1},a,b\}
.
By Definition
2.23
,
a\#_{1}^{l}b
,
e_{1}\leq_{1}^{l}a
,
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1}^{l}b
, and
e_{1}\#_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}_{1}
.
By Definition
2.14
,
a\#^{\prime}b
.

(ii)

a\#_{2}b
.

We then know that
(a,x)\#(b,x)
and that
a,b\in E_{2}
.
Clearly
(a,x),(b,x)\neq l
and
\neg((a,x),(b,x)\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
.
Furthermore,
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.
Thus
(a,x\backslash l)=f(a,x),(b,x\backslash l)=f(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.
It then follows by Definition
2.14
,
(a,x\backslash l)\#^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)
.

(iii)

a\#(b,x)

Then
e_{1}\leq_{1}a
, and
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq(b,x)
with
e^{\prime}_{1}\in x
.
We know that
e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}\neq l
since if
e^{\prime}_{1}=l
then
e^{\prime}_{1}\#_{1}e_{1}\leq_{1}a\Rightarrow e^{\prime}_{1}\#_{1}a
, which would entail
a\not\in E
, a contradiction.
Furthermore,
\neg(e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}\#_{1}l)
with similar arguments.
We know that
f(e)=e
for
e\in\{e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1},a\}
and
f(b,x)=(b,x\backslash l)
, since
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.
By Definition
2.23
,
a\#_{1}^{l}b
,
e_{1}\leq_{1}^{l}a
,
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1}^{l}b
, and
e_{1}\#_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}_{1}
.
By Definition
2.14
,
a\#^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)
.

f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b)
entails

\exists(f(e_{1})\leq^{\prime}f(a),f(e^{\prime}_{1})\leq^{\prime}f(b))\,.\,f(e_{1})\#_{1}^{l}f(e^{\prime}_{1})
or

f(a)\#_{2}f(b)
(note that
f(e_{1})=e_{1}
and
f(e^{\prime}_{1})=e^{\prime}_{1}
because

e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}\in E_{1}
). We have three cases:

(i)

f(a)\#_{1}^{l}f(b)
.

We know that for
e\in\{e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1},a,b\}
, we have
f(e)=e
,
e\leq l
, and
\neg(e\#_{1}l)
.
We then have
a\#_{1}^{l}b
.
By Definition
2.23
,
a\#_{1}b
,
e_{1}\leq_{1}a
,
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1}b
, and
e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}
.
By Definition
2.14
,
a\#_{1\,;\,2}b
,
e_{1}\leq_{1\,;\,2}a
,
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1\,;\,2}b
, and
e_{1}\#_{1\,;\,2}e^{\prime}_{1}
.
By Definition
2.23
,
a\#b
.

(ii)

f(a,x)\#^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

We know that
f(a,x)=(a,x\backslash l)
and
f(b,x)=(b,x\backslash l)
.
Then
(a,x\backslash l)\#^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)
.
By Definition
2.14
,
a\#_{2}b
.
Furthermore,
(x\backslash l)\cup\{l\}=x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
, since
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.
We then have
(a,x),(b,x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
and
\neg((a,x),(b,x)\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
, since
x
has no events in conflict with
l
.
By Definition
2.14
,
(a,x)\#(b,x)
.

(iii)

f(a)\#^{\prime}f(b,x)
.

This entails
f(e_{1})\leq^{\prime}f(a),f(e^{\prime}_{1})\leq^{\prime}f(b,x)\,.\,f(e_{1})\#_{1}^{l}f(e^{\prime}_{1})
.
We know that for
e\in\{e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1},a\}
, we have
f(e)=e
,
e\leq l
, and
\neg(e\#_{1}l)
.
Furthermore,
f(b,x)=(b,x\backslash l)
.
We then have
a\#^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)
and
e_{1}\leq^{\prime}a,e^{\prime}_{1}\leq^{\prime}(b,x\backslash l)\,.\,e_{1}\#_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}_{1}
.
By Definition
2.14
,
e_{1}\leq_{1}^{l}a
and
e^{\prime}_{1}\in x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.
By Definition
2.23
,
e_{1}\leq_{1}a
and
e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}
.
By Definition
2.14
,
e_{1}\leq_{1\,;\,2}a
and
e_{1}\#_{1\,;\,2}e^{\prime}_{1}
.
Furthermore,
(x\backslash l)\cup\{l\}=x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
, since
x\backslash l\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
.
Thus
e^{\prime}_{1}\in x
and, consequently,
e^{\prime}_{1}\leq_{1\,;\,2}(b,x)
.
Furthermore,
\neg((b,x)\#_{1\,;\,2}l)
since
x
has no events in conflict with
l
.
By Definition
2.23
,
a\#(b,x)
.

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}}\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,;\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l

1.

We start by defining
f:E^{\prime}\rightarrow E
such that

f(e)=\begin{cases}e&\text{ if }e\in E_{1}^{l}\\
(e,x\cup\{l\})&\text{ if }e\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)\end{cases}

It is straightforward to see that if
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)
then
x\cup\{l\}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
.

We now show that
f
is injective.
We have two cases:

–

a,b\in E_{1}^{l}

Then we are done since the mapping is the identity.

–

(a,x),(b,y)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)

From
(a,x)\neq(b,y)
we have two cases:

*

a\neq b

Trivially holds because the function is the identity.

*

a=b
and
x\neq y

Then

f(a,x)\neq f(b,y)\Leftrightarrow(a,x\cup\{l\})\neq(b,y\cup\{l\})\Leftrightarrow a\neq b\text{ or }x\cup\{l\}\neq y\cup\{l\}
.

Clearly it cannot be the case
a\neq b
, otherwise it would contradict our assumption
that
a=b
, and also the fact that the identity function is injective. Hence it can only
be
x\cup\{l\}\neq y\cup\{l\}
. Since
x\neq y
then
\exists e\in x
such that

e\not\in y
. Since
e\neq l
, then we have
x\cup\{l\}\neq y\cup\{l\}
.

•

\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)

Similar to the other case

•

a\leq^{\prime}b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\leq f(b)

Similar to the other case

•

a\#^{\prime}b\Leftrightarrow f(a)\#f(b)

Similar to the other case

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.30

Proof.

We need to prove

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,\square\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l
,

\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,\square\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l
when

l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{1}})

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,\square\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{2}\backslash l
,

\mathrm{E}_{2}\backslash l\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,\square\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l
when

l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{2}})

Since both cases are identical, we focus solely when
l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{1}})
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,\square\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{E}_{1}\,\square\,\mathrm{E}_{2})\backslash l=(E^{\prime},\,\leq^{\prime},\,\#^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l=(E_{1}^{l},\,\leq_{1}^{l},\,\#_{1}^{l})

\displaystyle l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,\square\,\mathrm{E_{2}})

Consider
l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{1}})
.

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,\square\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\sqsubseteq\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l

1.

We begin by defining the function
f:E^{\prime}\rightarrow E_{1}^{l}
as the identity, which is
injective.

2.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)

It follows directly since
f
is the identity

3.

e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e\leq_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}

\Rightarrow

Assume
e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}
.

By Definition
2.23
,
e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}
entails
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
. From
f
we know
that
e,e^{\prime}\not\in E_{2}
. Hence
\neg(e\leq_{2}e^{\prime})
. Thus it only remains that

e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
with
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
. Again, from
f
we know that
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{l}
since

e,e^{\prime}\neq l
and
\neg(e\#l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#l)
. Thus, by Definition
2.23
,

e\leq_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
e\leq_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}
.

By Definition
2.23
,
e\leq_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}
entails
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
with

e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{l}
. Hence
e,e^{\prime}\neq l
,
\neg(e\#_{1}l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#_{1}l)
, and

e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
. By Definition
2.18
we have
e,e^{\prime}\in E
and
e\leq e^{\prime}
. Furthermore, we also have
\neg(e\#l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#l)
and consequently

e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
. Thus
e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}
.

4.

e\#^{\prime}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e\#_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}

\Rightarrow

Assume
e\#^{\prime}e^{\prime}
.

By Definition
2.23
we have
e\#e^{\prime}
and
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
such that

e,e^{\prime}\neq l
and
\neg(e\#l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#l)
. By Definition
2.18
,

e\#e^{\prime}
entails
e\#_{1}e^{\prime}
or
e\#_{2}e^{\prime}
or
\set{e\#e^{\prime}\mid e\in E_{1},e^{\prime}\in E_{2}}
.
Since
l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
, then
l\in E_{1}
and consequently
e,e^{\prime}\not\in E_{2}
. Thus,
by exclusion of hypothesis we have
e\#_{1}e^{\prime}
and consequently

\neg(e\#_{1}l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#_{1}l)
. Thus by Definition
2.23
we have

e\#_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
e\#_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}
.

By Definition
2.23
we have
e\#_{1}e^{\prime}
and
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{l}
, such that

e,e^{\prime}\neq l
and
\neg(e\#_{1}l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#_{1}l)
. Furthermore
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
. By
Definition
2.18
,
e,e^{\prime}\in E
and
\neg(e\#l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#l)
. By
Definition
2.23
we have
e\#^{\prime}e^{\prime}
.

•

\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,\square\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l

For this case, we notice that the function
f:E^{\prime}\rightarrow E
is again the identity, which
is injective.

We omit the remaining cases since they are similarly proved.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.31

Proof.

It is straightforward to see that if
l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}})
then either

l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
or
l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E}_{2})
, by Definition
2.16
. Let us
focus in the case where
l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E}_{1})
, since the proof for the other way around is
similar.

We need to prove

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}}

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}}\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{E}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{E}_{2})\backslash l=(E^{\prime},\,\leq^{\prime},\,\#^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l=(E_{1}^{l},\,\leq_{1}^{l},\,\#_{1}^{l})

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{2}\backslash l=(E_{2}^{l},\,\leq_{2}^{l},\,\#_{2}^{l})

\displaystyle(\mathrm{E}_{1}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E^{l},\,\leq^{l},\,\#^{l})

\displaystyle l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}})

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}}

1.

We start by defining
f:E^{\prime}\rightarrow E^{l}
as being the identity, which is injective.

2.

\pi(f(a))=\pi(a)

It follows directly since
f
is the identity

3.

e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e\leq^{l}e^{\prime}

\Rightarrow

Assume
e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}

By Definition
2.23
,
e\leq e^{\prime}
and
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
.
We have two cases:

(i)

e\leq e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}

Then
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
. Since
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
, then
e,e^{\prime}\neq l
and

\neg(e\#l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#l)
. By Definition
2.16

\neg(e\#_{1}l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#_{1}l)
. By Definition
2.23
,
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{l}

and
e\leq_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}
. By Definition
2.16
,
e\leq^{l}e^{\prime}
.

(ii)

e\leq e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}

It follows directly that
e\leq^{l}e^{\prime}
, since
l\in E_{1}
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
e\leq^{l}e^{\prime}

By Definition
2.23
and by Definition
2.16
we have two cases:

(i)

e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{l}
and
e\leq^{l}e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}

Since
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{l}
, by Definition
2.23
we have
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
,

e,e^{\prime}\neq l
and
\neg(e\#_{1}l),\neg(e^{\prime}\#_{1}l)
. Hence, by
Definition
2.16
and Definition
2.23
we have
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
.
From
e\leq_{1}^{l}e^{\prime}
, we know that from Definition
2.23
we have

e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
and
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{l}
. It then follows by Definition
2.16
and
Definition
2.23
that
e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}
.

(ii)

e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2}^{l}
and
e\leq^{l}e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq_{2}^{l}e^{\prime}

It follows directly that
e\leq^{\prime}e^{\prime}
, since
l\in E_{1}
.

4.

e\#^{\prime}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e\#^{l}e^{\prime}

The reasoning for this case is similar to the previous one, since the definitions are
identical.

•

(\mathrm{E_{1}}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}}\sqsubseteq(\mathrm{E_{1}}\,||\,\mathrm{E_{2}})\backslash l

For this case, we notice that the function
f:E^{l}\rightarrow E^{\prime}
is again the identity,
which is injective.

We omit the remaining cases since they are similarly proved.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.32

Proof.

It follows directly from Definition
2.16
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.33

Proof.

Induction over rules in Figure
4
.

•

skip\xrightarrow{sk}\checkmark

It follows directly that

\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket skip\rrbracket\backslash sk\equiv\emptyset
.

•

a\xrightarrow{a}\checkmark

It follows directly that

\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket a\rrbracket\backslash a\equiv\emptyset
.

•

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{2}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:op-sim-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket,\text{ Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-rem-init1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:op-sim-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-rem-init1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark\text{ or }C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\text{ or }\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:nd-rem-init1} for both cases,
\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}
}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\text{ or }C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\text{ or }\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:nd-rem-init1} for both cases},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l\text{ or }\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{2}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:op-sim-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init1}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,C_{2}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:op-sim-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init1}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:op-sim-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init1}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:op-sim-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init1}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.34

Proof.

Induction over the length of
\omega
, which is denoted by
|\omega|
.

•

|\omega|=1

It follows directly that

\exists\set{l}\in\mathcal{C}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)\,.\,\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{l}

•

|\omega|>1

\displaystyle C\xrightarrow{\omega}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{fig:op-nstep1}}}

\displaystyle C\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime\prime}\qquad C^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\omega^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{res:soundI-1}, i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C\rrbracket\backslash l\qquad\exists y\in\mathcal{C}(\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)\,.\,\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ y

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:rem-init1}}}

\displaystyle\set{l}\cup y\in\mathcal{C}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)\,.\,\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{l}\cup y=x

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.35

Proof.

Induction over the interpretation of commands.

•

sk\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket skip\rrbracket)

Let
C^{\prime}=\checkmark
. It follows directly that
skip\xrightarrow{sk}\checkmark

and that
\llbracket skip\rrbracket\backslash sk\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
.

•

a\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket a\rrbracket)

Let
C^{\prime}=\checkmark
. It follows directly that
a\xrightarrow{a}\checkmark

and that
\llbracket a\rrbracket\backslash a\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
.

•

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

By Definition
2.14
we have that
l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)
. By i.h.,

\exists C^{\prime}
such that
C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l=\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket
. We have two cases:

1.

C^{\prime}=\checkmark

We have
C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure
4
,

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{2}
.
By Definition
2.14
,
(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.

2.

C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{1}

We have
C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure
4
,

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}
. By
Definition
2.14
,

(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.
By Definition
2.20
,
\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket
.

•

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

By Definition
2.18
we have two cases:

1.

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)

By i.h.
\exists C^{\prime}\,.\,C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
By the rules in Figure
4
we have two cases:

(a)

C^{\prime}=\checkmark

We have
C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure
4

we have
C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark
. By
Lemma
2.30
we have

\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
.
By Definition
2.20
,
\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

(b)

C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{1}

We have
C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure

we have
C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}
. By
Lemma
2.30
we have

\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
. By Definition
2.20
,

\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

2.

By i.h.
\exists C^{\prime}\,.\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}
and

\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
By the rules in Figure
4
we have two cases:

(a)

C^{\prime}=\checkmark

We have
C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark
and

\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure
4

we have
C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark
. By

2.30
we have

\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
.
By Definition
2.20
,
\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

(b)

C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{2}

We have
C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{2}
and

\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure
4

we have
C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{2}
. By
Lemma
2.30
we have

\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
.
By Definition
2.20
,
\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

•

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

2.16
we have two cases:

.

. By

,

(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.

.

Proof of Theorem
2.36

Proof.

Induction over the length of
\omega
.

•

|\omega|=1

We have
\set{l}\in\mathcal{C}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
such that
\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{l}
.
Furthermore
l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
. By Lemma
2.35
,

C\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}
and

\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C\rrbracket\backslash l
. By the rules in
Figure
5
,
C\xrightarrow{l}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\prime}
.

•

|\omega|>1

We have
x\in\mathcal{C}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
such that
\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ x
. Since

\omega=l_{0}l_{1}\dots l_{n}
, then
\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l_{0}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{l_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ x
. Hence

l_{0}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
. By Lemma
2.35
,

C\xrightarrow{l_{0}}C^{\prime}
and

\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C\rrbracket\backslash l_{0}
. By
Definition
2.23
,
\exists y\in\mathcal{C}(\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket)
such that

\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ y
. By i.h.
\exists C^{\prime\prime}
such that

C^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\omega^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
. By the rules in Figure
5
,

C\xrightarrow{\omega}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
, where
\omega=l_{0}:\omega^{\prime}
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.40

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{E}_{1}
,
\mathrm{E}_{2}
, and
\mathrm{E}_{3}
be event structures.

•

Reflexivity:
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{1}

It follows directly from Definition
2.39
.

•

Transitivity:
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2},\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{3}\Rightarrow\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{3}

1.

E_{1}\subseteq E_{3}

Let
e\in E_{1}
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
then
e\in E_{2}
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{3}
then
e\in E_{3}
. Hence
E_{1}\subseteq E_{3}
.

2.

e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1},e\leq_{3}e^{\prime}

\Rightarrow

Let
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
.

Clearly
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
then
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
. Furthermore
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{3}
then

e\leq_{3}e^{\prime}
.

\Leftarrow

Let
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1},e\leq_{3}e^{\prime}
.

Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
then
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{3}
then
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}

e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
.

3.

e\#_{1}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1},e\#_{3}e^{\prime}

Similar to
\leq
,
i.e.
previous point.

•

Antisymmetry:
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2},\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{1}\Rightarrow\mathrm{E}_{1}=\mathrm{E}_{2}

1.

E_{1}=E_{2}

Let
e\in E_{1}
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
then
e\in E_{2}
. Let
e\in E_{2}
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{1}
then
e\in E_{1}
. Hence
E_{1}=E_{2}
.

2.

(e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1},e\leq_{2}e^{\prime})=(e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2},e\leq_{1}e^{\prime})

\Rightarrow

Let
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
. Clearly
e,e^{\prime}\in\mathrm{E}_{1}
. From

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
,
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
.

Let
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
. Clearly
e,e^{\prime}\in\mathrm{E}_{2}
. From

\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{1}
,
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
.

\Leftarrow

Let
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1},e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
then
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
. Let
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2},e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{1}
then
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
.

Hence
\leq_{1}=\leq_{2}

3.

(e\#_{1}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1},e\#_{2}e^{\prime})=(e\#_{2}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2},e\#_{1}e^{\prime})

Similar reasoning as previous point.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.41

Proof.

•

\bot
is an event structure

It follows directly that all conditions in Definition
2.1
are trivially satisfied
because
\bot
has no events.

•

For any event structure
\mathrm{E}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)
we want to show

\bot\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}
.

1.

\emptyset\subseteq E

Trivially holds.

2.

e\leq_{\bot}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in\emptyset,e\leq e^{\prime}

Since
\bot
has no events and the causal relation is empty, it follows that
e\leq_{\bot}e^{\prime}

and
e,e^{\prime}\in\emptyset
are false. Hence the condition trivially holds.

3.

e\#_{\bot}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in\emptyset,e\#e^{\prime}

Similar to previous point.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.43

Proof.

It follows directly from Definition
2.42
that
\leq^{\omega}
is a partial order and
that
\#^{\omega}
is symmetric and irreflexive, since for every case, it exists
n\in\omega

such that
\leq^{\omega}=\leq_{n}
and
\#^{\omega}=\#_{n}
, with
\leq_{n}
being a partial order
and
\#_{n}
being symmetric and irreflexive.

•

\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq^{\omega}e}
is finite

By Definition
2.42
,
e^{\prime}\leq^{\omega}e^{\prime}
entails
\exists n\in\omega
such that

e^{\prime}\leq_{n}e
. Consequently,
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{n}
. Furthermore,
\mathrm{E}_{n}
is an event
structure. Hence
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq_{n}e}
is finite. Thus
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq^{\omega}e}

is finite.

•

e\#^{\omega}e^{\prime}\leq^{\omega}e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow e\#^{\omega}e^{\prime\prime}

By Definition
2.42
,
e^{\prime}\leq^{\omega}e^{\prime}
entails
\exists n\in\omega
such that

e\#_{n}e^{\prime}\leq_{n}e^{\prime\prime}
, where
\mathrm{E}_{n}
is an event structure. Hence
e\#_{n}e^{\prime\prime}
. Thus

e\#^{\omega}e^{\prime\prime}
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.44

Proof.

•

\mathrm{E}^{\omega}
is an upper bound

\forall n\in\omega
we need to have
\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\omega}
. It follows
directly from Definition
2.39
that

\forall n\in\omega\,\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\omega}
since
\mathrm{E}^{\omega}
is by
definition the union of all
\mathrm{E}_{n}
.

•

\mathrm{E}^{\omega}
is the least upper bound

Let
\mathrm{E}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)
be an upper bound of the chain. We need to show that if

\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\omega}
and
\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}
then

\mathrm{E}^{\omega}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}
.

1.

E^{\omega}\subseteq E

Let
e\in E^{\omega}
. By Definition
2.42
,
\exists n\in\omega
such that

e\in E_{n}
. By
\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}
we have
e\in E
.

2.

e\leq^{\omega}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\omega}
and
e\leq e^{\prime}

\Rightarrow

Let
e\leq^{\omega}e^{\prime}
.

By Definition
2.42
,
\exists n\in\omega
such that
e\leq_{n}e^{\prime}
. It is then
clear that
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{n}\subseteq E^{\omega}
. Since
\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}
we
have
e\leq e^{\prime}
.

\Leftarrow

e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\omega}
and
e\leq e^{\prime}

By Definition
2.42
,
\exists n\in\omega
such that
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{n}
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}
,
e\leq_{n}e^{\prime}
. By Definition
2.42
,

e\leq^{\omega}e^{\prime}
.

3.

e\#^{\omega}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E^{\omega}
and
e\#e^{\prime}

Similar to previous point.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.46

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{E}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#)
,
\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1})
,

\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2})
,
\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{1}=(E^{1},\,\leq^{1},\,\#^{1})
, and

\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{2}=(E^{2},\,\leq^{2},\,\#^{2})
, such that
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
.

1.

E^{1}\subseteq E^{2}\Leftrightarrow E\uplus(E_{1}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}))\subseteq E\uplus(E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E}))

By Definition
2.14
we have two cases:

(a)

e\in E

Then we are done.

(b)

e\in E_{1}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})

We know that
e
is of the form
(e_{1},x)
where
e_{1}\in E_{1}
and
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
.
Since
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
then
e_{1}\in E_{2}
and consequently

e\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
.

2.

\forall e,e^{\prime}\ .\ e\leq^{1}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
and
e\leq^{2}e^{\prime}

\Rightarrow

Assume
e\leq^{1}e
.
Clearly
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
.
By Definition
2.14
we have three cases:

(a)

e\leq^{1}e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq e^{\prime}

Hence
e,e^{\prime}\in E
. By Definition
2.14

e\leq^{2}e^{\prime}
.

(b)

e\leq e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}

We know that
e,e^{\prime}
are of the form
(e,x),(e^{\prime},x)\in E_{1}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
, which
entails
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
and
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
,
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
and
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
. By
Definition
2.14
we have
(e,x)\leq^{2}(e^{\prime},x)
.

(c)

e\leq^{1}e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq^{1}(e^{\prime},x)

We know that
e\in E
,
e\in x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
, and

(e^{\prime},x)\in E_{1}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(es{E})
, with the last entailing
e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
,
e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
and consequently

(e^{\prime},x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
. By Definition
2.14
we have

e\leq^{2}(e^{\prime},x)
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
and
e\leq^{2}e^{\prime}
. The cases are distinguished by

\leq^{2}
.

(a)

e\leq^{2}e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq e^{\prime}

Hence
e,e^{\prime}\in E
. By Definition
2.14
,
e\leq^{1}e^{\prime}
.

(b)

e\leq^{2}e
is of the form
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}

We know that
e,e^{\prime}
are of the form
(e,x),(e^{\prime},x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
, which
entails
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
and
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
and
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
, which entails for this case that

e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
, then
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
. By Definition
2.14
we have

(e,x)\leq^{1}(e^{\prime},x)
.

(c)

e\leq^{2}e^{\prime}
is of the form
e\leq^{2}(e^{\prime},x)

We know that
e\in E
,
e\in x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
, and

(e^{\prime},x)\in E_{2}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(es{E})
, with the last entailing
e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
. Since

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
and
e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
, which entails for this case that

e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
, then
(e^{\prime},x)\in E_{1}\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
. By
Definition
2.14
we have
e\leq^{1}(e^{\prime},x)
.

3.

\forall e,e^{\prime}\ .\ e\#^{1}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e,e^{\prime}\in E
and
e\#^{2}e^{\prime}

\Rightarrow

Assume
e\#^{1}e^{\prime}
.

Clearly
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
. From Definition
2.14
we have that

\exists(a\leq^{1}e,\ a^{\prime}\leq^{1}e^{\prime})
such that
a\#a^{\prime}
or
e\#_{1}e^{\prime}
. For the former we
have that
a\leq^{1}e,\ a^{\prime}\leq^{1}e^{\prime}
entails
a\leq^{2}e,\ a^{\prime}\leq^{2}e^{\prime}
. For the latter we
have that
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
, hence
e\#_{2}e^{\prime}
. Thus
e\#^{2}e^{\prime}
.

\Leftarrow

Assume
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
and
e\#^{2}e^{\prime}
.

By Definition
2.14
it exists
a\leq^{2}e
and
a^{\prime}\leq^{2}e^{\prime}
such that
a\#a^{\prime}

or
e\#_{2}e^{\prime}
. Since
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{2}
we have

a\leq^{1}e,\ a^{\prime}\leq^{1}e^{\prime}
and
e\#_{1}e^{\prime}
. It then follows directly that
e\#^{1}e^{\prime}
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.47

Proof.

It follows directly from Definition
2.16
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.48

Proof.

It follows directly from Definition
2.18
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.49

Proof.

•

\Rightarrow
: Assume
op
is continuous.

We have
op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})=\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}_{n})
. Let

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\dots
and

\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n}\trianglelefteq\dots
be two

\omega
-chains such that

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n}
. We want to show
that

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}\Rightarrow op\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq op\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n}\Rightarrow op\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq op\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n},\dots

For that we can make use of the least upper bound,
i.e.

\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n}\Rightarrow op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})\trianglelefteq\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n})
. Since
op
is continuous,

op\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\bigsqcup_{n}op\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n}
. Hence
op
is monotonic. Now
it lacks to show that each event of
op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})
is an event of

\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{n})
. But that comes freely since

op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})=\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}_{n})
.

•

\Leftarrow
: Assume
1.
and
2.
above.

We want to show
op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})=\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}_{n})
. Let

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\dots
be a

\omega
-chain. By
1.
we know that
op
is monotonic, hence

\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n}
entails

op(\mathrm{E}_{n})\trianglelefteq op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{N})
that leads to

op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})\trianglelefteq\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}_{n})
. By
2.
, each event of

op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})
is an event of
\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}_{n})
. Hence by
Definition
2.39
,
op(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})=\bigsqcup_{n}op(\mathrm{E}_{n})
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.50

Proof.

By Lemma
2.46
we know that sequential composition is monotone w.r.t

\trianglelefteq
at right. It lacks to show that each event of

\mathrm{E}\,;\,\bigsqcup_{m}\mathrm{E}_{m}
is an event of
\bigsqcup_{m}(\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{m})
. Let

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{m}\trianglelefteq\dots
be an

\omega
-chain such that
\bigsqcup_{m}\mathrm{E}_{m}
is its least upper bound and

\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{m}\trianglelefteq\dots
be another
\omega
-chain with
\bigsqcup_{m}(\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{m})
as its
least upper bound. Let
e
be an event of
\mathrm{E}\,;\,\bigsqcup_{m}\mathrm{E}_{m}
. By
Definition
2.14
we have two cases:

1.

e
is an event of
\mathrm{E}

Then we are done, since
\forall m
,
e
is an event of
\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{m}
. Hence it is an
event of
\bigsqcup_{m}(\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{m})
.

2.

e
is an event of
(\bigcup_{m\in\omega}E_{m})\times\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})

We know that
e
is of the form
(e_{m},x)
with
e_{m}
an event of
\bigsqcup_{m}\mathrm{E}_{m}
and

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{E})
. The former entails
\exists m
such that
e_{m}
is an event of

\mathrm{E}_{m}
. By Definition
2.14
we have
(e_{m},x)
as an event of

\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{m}
. Consequently
(e_{m},x)
is an event of

\bigsqcup_{m}(\mathrm{E}\,;\,\mathrm{E}_{m})
.

By Lemma
2.49
we are done.
∎

2.51

Proof.

2.47
we know that parallel composition is monotone w.r.t

\trianglelefteq
. It lacks to show that each event of

\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n}\,||\,\bigsqcup_{m}\mathrm{E}_{m}

\bigsqcup_{n,m}(\mathrm{E}_{n}\,||\,\mathrm{E}_{m})

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\dots

e

.

2.16

2.16
,

we are done.

Proof.

we know that non-deterministic composition is monotone w.r.t

\trianglelefteq
. It lacks to show that each event of

\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n}\,\square\,\bigsqcup_{m}\mathrm{E}_{m}
is an event of

\bigsqcup_{n,m}(\mathrm{E}_{n}\,\square\,\mathrm{E}_{m})

\mathrm{E}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}_{n}\trianglelefteq\dots
and

\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1}\trianglelefteq\dots\trianglelefteq\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{m}\trianglelefteq\dots

\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n}
and
\bigsqcup_{m}\mathrm{E}_{m}
, respectively.

Proof of Lemma
2.53

Proof.

\Gamma(fix(\Gamma))=fix(\Gamma)\Leftrightarrow\Gamma(\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\bot))=\bigsqcup_{n}(\Gamma^{n}(\bot))
. Since
\Gamma
is continuous, we have that

\Gamma(\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\bot))=\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma\Gamma^{n}(\bot)=\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n+1}(\bot)
. We note that:

\bot\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma\Gamma^{n}(\bot)=\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\bot)
. Since
\bot
is
the ‘identity of the least upper bound’ we have:

\bot\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma\Gamma^{n}(\bot)=\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\bot)\Leftrightarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma\Gamma^{n}(\bot)=\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\bot)\Leftrightarrow\Gamma(\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\bot))=\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\bot)
.

Now we need to show that
fix(\Gamma)
is the least fixpoint. Let
E
be an event structure,

\Gamma(E)\trianglelefteq E
, and
\perp\trianglelefteq E
. By the monotonic property

\Gamma(\perp)\trianglelefteq\Gamma(E)
. Since
\Gamma(E)\trianglelefteq E
then

\Gamma(\perp)\trianglelefteq E
. By induction
\Gamma^{n}(E)\trianglelefteq E
. Thus

fix(\Gamma)=\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{n}(\perp)\trianglelefteq E
. Hence
fix(\Gamma)
is the least
fixpoint.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.55

Proof.

•

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C_{1}\,;\,C_{2},\gamma}(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{C_{2},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-cont-1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{n}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\Gamma^{C_{2},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{n}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})}

•

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C_{1}\,||\,C_{2},\gamma}(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{C_{1},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})\,||\,\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{C_{2},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-cont-1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{n}(\Gamma^{C_{1},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})\,||\,\Gamma^{C_{2},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{n}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})}\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})}

•

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2},\gamma}(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{C_{1},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})\,\square\,\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{C_{2},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:nd-cont-1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{n}(\Gamma^{C_{1},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})\,\square\,\Gamma^{C_{2},\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{n}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})}\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})}

•

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\mu X.C,\gamma}(\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle fix(\Gamma^{C,\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})})=fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\Gamma^{C,\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n})}(\mathrm{E}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{n}\mathrm{E}_{n},Y\leftarrow E)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\bigsqcup_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n},Y\leftarrow\mathrm{E})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{abramsky94}{}{}, Proposition~2.1.18]}}}

\displaystyle fix(\bigsqcup_{n}(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n},Y\leftarrow\mathrm{E})}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{$fix$ continuous}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n},Y\leftarrow\mathrm{E})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\Gamma^{C,\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})}(\mathrm{E}))=\bigsqcup_{n}fix(\Gamma^{C,\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mathrm{E}_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{n}\Gamma^{\mu X.C,\gamma}(\mathrm{E}_{n})

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.56

Proof.

•

\llbracket skip[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

It follows directly that
\llbracket skip\rrbracket_{\gamma{(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}}
.

•

\llbracket a[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

It follows directly that
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\gamma{(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}}
.

•

\displaystyle\llbracket(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{fig:substitution-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,(C_{2}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}])\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

•

\displaystyle\llbracket(C_{1}\,||\,C_{2})[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{fig:substitution-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\,||\,C_{2}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

•

\displaystyle\llbracket(C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2})[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{fig:substitution-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\,\square\,C_{2}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}\,\square\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,\square\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

•

\displaystyle\llbracket(\mu Y.C^{\prime})[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{fig:substitution-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\mu Y.(C^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}])\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle fix(\Gamma^{C^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}],\gamma})=fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\Gamma^{C^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}],\gamma}(\mathrm{E}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\llbracket C^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma(Y\leftarrow\mathrm{E})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\gamma(Y\leftarrow\mathrm{E},X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle fix(\mathrm{E}\mapsto\Gamma^{C^{\prime},\gamma(Y\leftarrow\mathrm{E},X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}(\mathrm{E}))=fix(\Gamma^{C^{\prime},\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}\,;\,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mu Y.C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.57

Proof.

\displaystyle\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

Definition
2.54

\displaystyle fix(\Gamma^{C,\gamma})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:fix-prop-1}}}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C,\gamma}(fix(\Gamma^{C,\gamma}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C,\gamma}(\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:fix-den-sem-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.59

Proof.

\displaystyle\mu X.C

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small1} entails}}

\displaystyle C\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\gamma}\equiv\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma}\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{setting }\gamma=\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}
