\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}\equiv\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:1-1}, Lemma~\ref{lem:2-1}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}]\rrbracket_{\gamma}\equiv\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma}\backslash l

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.60

Proof.

•

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})

By Definition
2.54
and Definition
2.42
,

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma})
. By i.h.,
\exists C^{\prime}
such that

C\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}
and

\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\gamma}\equiv\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma}\backslash l
. By the rules in
Figure
4
and by setting

\gamma=\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})
,

\mu X.C\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\mu X.C]
and

\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\llbracket\mu X.C\rrbracket_{\gamma})}\equiv\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\gamma(X\leftarrow\mu X.C)}\backslash l

∎

Proofs of Section
3

Remark 6
.

Here we may found useful to write the sum in Equation
1
in another way.
Concretely:

\displaystyle v(y)-\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n}}(-1)^{|I|+1}v\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)=\sum_{I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n}}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)

Proof of Lemma
3.6

Proof.

We begin by showing that
v(x)
is well-defined,
i.e.
that given

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that
x=x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})
and

x=x^{\prime}_{1}\cup(x^{\prime}_{2}\times\{x^{\prime}_{1}\})
then
x_{1}=x^{\prime}_{1}
and
x_{2}=x^{\prime}_{2}
.

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
,
x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
,

x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
, and

x=x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})=x^{\prime}_{1}\cup(x^{\prime}_{2}\times\{x^{\prime}_{1}\})
.

We start by showing that
x_{1}=x^{\prime}_{1}
. Since
x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
, by
Lemma
2.7
, distinct maximal configurations of
\mathrm{P}_{1}
have events
in conflict:

x_{1}\neq x^{\prime}_{1}\Rightarrow\exists e1\in x_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}\in x^{\prime}_{1}\text{ such that }e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}

By contradiction, assume that
x_{1}\neq x^{\prime}_{1}
. Since maximal configurations have events in
conflict, then there exist
e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}
such that
e_{1}\#_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}
. Furthermore, we have that

x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq x
. Hence
e_{1},e^{\prime}_{1}\in x
. However, by Definition
3.5
, the
conflict relation
\#
restricts to
\#_{1}
on events from
E_{1}
. Thus
e_{1}\#e^{\prime}_{1}
. Since
x

contains events in conflict, it contradicts that
x
is a configuration (configurations are
conflict-free by definition). Therefore,
x_{1}=x^{\prime}_{1}
.

We now show that
x_{2}=x^{\prime}_{2}
. We already know that
x_{1}=x^{\prime}_{1}
. Hence we can rewrite

x=x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})=x_{1}\cup(x^{\prime}_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})
. Now, we only need to show
that
x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\}=x^{\prime}_{2}\times\{x_{1}\}
. For that, consider the map

f_{x_{1}}:\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})\rightarrow\mathrm{P}({\mathrm{E}_{2}\times\{x_{1}\}}),\ x_{2}\mapsto x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\}
. Such map is injective (since we pair each configuration
x_{2}
with the maximal
configuration
x_{1}
). Hence, it follows from injectivity that
x_{2}=x^{\prime}_{2}
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

By Lemma
2.15
we know that
\mathrm{E}
is an event structure.
Hence we focus solely on the valuation part.

1.

v(\emptyset)=1

Since
\emptyset\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
then
v(\emptyset)=v_{1}(\emptyset)=1
.

2.

d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0
for all
n\geq 1
and

y,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
with
y\subseteq x_{1},\dots,x_{n}

We have two cases based on
n
:

(a)

n=0

We have
d^{(0)}_{v}[y\,;\,]=v(y)
.
By Definition
3.5
we have two cases:

i.

y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that
y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})

It follows directly that
v(y)=v_{1}(y)\geq 0
.

ii.

y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that
y=y_{1}\cup(y_{2}\times\{y_{1}\})

with
y_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1}),y_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
.

It follows directly that
v(y)=v_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v_{2}(y_{2})\geq 0
, since
v_{1},v_{2}
are
valuations.

(b)

n>0

By
[
19
, Proposition 5]
we only need to check the condition for

y\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}\ x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.
We have three cases:

i.

y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
but
y\not\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1})

By
[
19
, Proposition 5]
we know that
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
,
since
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \
is a “single-step” relation. We have

y,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
. It follows directly that

d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]=d^{(n)}_{v_{1}}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0
.

ii.

y\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1})

By
[
19
, Proposition 5]
we know that

x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\not\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
, since
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \
is a “single-step”
relation. Hence exists
x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

x_{1}=y\cup(x^{\prime}_{1}\times\{y\}),\dots,x_{n}=y\cup(x^{\prime}_{n}\times\{y\})
. Furthermore

\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}=y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}(x^{\prime}_{i}\times\{y\})
and let

I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n}
.

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}(y\cup(x^{\prime}_{i}\times\{y\}))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}(x^{\prime}_{i}\times\{y\})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v_{1}(y)\cdot v_{2}\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}x^{\prime}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=v_{1}(y)\cdot\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v_{2}\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}x^{\prime}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=v_{1}(y)\cdot d^{(n)}_{v_{2}}[\emptyset\,;\,x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n}]

Since
v_{1}(y)\geq 0
and
d^{(n)}_{v_{2}}[\emptyset\,;\,x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n}]\geq 0
, then

v_{1}(y)\cdot d^{(n)}_{v_{2}}[\emptyset\,;\,x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n}]\geq 0
.

iii.

y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
but
y\not\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
.

By
[
19
, Proposition 5]
we know that
\exists y_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
and

y^{\prime},x^{\prime}_{1},\dots x^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

y=y_{1}\cup(y^{\prime}\times\{y_{1}\}),x_{1}=y_{1}\cup(x^{\prime}_{1}\times\{y_{1}\}),\dots,x_{n}=y_{1}\cup(x^{\prime}_{n}\times\{y_{1}\})
, since
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \
is a “single-step” relation. Furthermore

\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}=\bigcup_{i\in I}(y_{1}\cup(x^{\prime}_{i}\times\{y_{1}\}))=y_{1}\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}(x^{\prime}_{i}\times\{y_{1}\})
and let
I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n}
.

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left((y_{1}\cup(y^{\prime}\times\{y_{1}\}))\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}(y_{1}\cup(x^{\prime}_{i}\times\{y_{1}\}))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y_{1}\cup\left((y^{\prime}\times\{y_{1}\})\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}(x^{\prime}_{i}\times\{y_{1}\})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v_{2}\left(y^{\prime}\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x^{\prime}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=v_{1}(y)\cdot\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v_{2}\left(y^{\prime}\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x^{\prime}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=v_{1}(y)\cdot d^{(n)}_{v_{2}}[y^{\prime}\,;\,x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n}]

Since
v_{1}(y)\geq 0
and
d^{(n)}_{v_{2}}[y^{\prime}\,;\,x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n}]\geq 0
, then

v_{1}(y)\cdot d^{(n)}_{v_{2}}[y^{\prime}\,;\,x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n}]\geq 0
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.8

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(E_{1},\,\leq_{1},\,\#_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(E_{2},\,\leq_{2},\,\#_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(E,\,\leq,\,\#,\,v)

We need to show that
\leq
is a partial order and that
\#
is symmetric and irreflexive.

•

\leq
is a partial order:

–

Reflexivity (
e\leq e
): we have the following cases:

1.

Case
e=\tau
. Then we are done since
\tau\leq\tau
.

2.

Case
e_{1}\leq_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}
or
e_{2}\leq_{2}e^{\prime}_{2}
. Since
\leq_{1}
and
\leq_{2}
are partial
orders, we are done.

–

Transitivity (
e\leq e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\leq e^{\prime\prime}
then
e\leq e^{\prime\prime}
): we have the following
cases:

1.

Case
e=\tau
and (
e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E_{1}
or
e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E_{2}
). Then
\tau\leq e^{\prime}
and

e^{\prime}\leq e^{\prime\prime}
. Since
e^{\prime\prime}\in E
the
\tau\leq e^{\prime\prime}
.

2.

Case (
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\leq_{1}e^{\prime\prime}
) or (
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\leq_{2}e^{\prime\prime}
). Since

\leq_{1}
and
\leq_{2}
are partial orders, we have
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime\prime}
or
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime\prime}
. Hence

e\leq e^{\prime\prime}
.

–

Antisymmetry (
e\leq e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\leq e
then
e^{\prime}=e
): we have the following cases:

1.

Case (
e\leq_{1}e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\leq_{1}e
) or (
e\leq_{2}e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\leq_{2}e
). Since

\leq_{1}
and
\leq_{2}
are partial orders, we have
e=e^{\prime}
.

2.

Case
e=\tau
and (
e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
or
e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
). We are done since
e^{\prime}\leq\tau
is
not possible.

Hence
\leq
is a partial order.

•

\#
is symmetric and irreflexive:

–

Symmetric (if
e\#e^{\prime}
then
e^{\prime}\#e
): we have the following cases

1.

if
e\#_{1}e^{\prime}
or
e\#_{2}e^{\prime}
. Since
\#_{1}
and
\#_{2}
are symmetric then we have

e^{\prime}\#_{1}e
or
e^{\prime}\#_{2}e
. Thus
e^{\prime}\#e
.

2.

if
e\in E_{1}
and
e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
, or vice-versa, then by Definition
3.7

we also have
e^{\prime}\leq e
.

–

Irreflexive (
\neg(e\#e)
): We have either
\neg(e\#_{1}e)
or
\neg(e\#_{2}e)
. Since

\#_{1}
and
\#_{2}
are irreflexive then
\neg(e\#e)
. There are no more cases since either

e\in E_{1}
or
e\in E_{2}
.

Hence
\#
is symmetric and irreflexive.

Let
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E
. We have four conditions to check:

1.

\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq e}
is finite

We have three cases:

(a)

e=\tau

It follows directly that
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq\tau}=\set{\tau}
since

\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
.

(b)

e\in E_{1}

We have that
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq e}=\set{\tau}\cup\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq_{1}e}
. Since

\mathrm{P}_{1}
is a probabilistic event structure, then we know that
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq_{1}e}

is finite. Hence
\set{\tau}\cup\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq_{1}e}
is finite.

(c)

e\in E_{2}

We have that
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq e}=\set{\tau}\cup\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq_{2}e}
. Since

\mathrm{P}_{2}
is a probabilistic event structure, then we know that
\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq_{2}e}

is finite. Hence
\set{\tau}\cup\set{e^{\prime}\mid e^{\prime}\leq_{2}e}
is finite.

2.

e\#e^{\prime}\leq e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow e\#e^{\prime\prime}

Since
\tau
is not in conflict with any event, this condition trivially holds because we either
have
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E_{1}
or
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in E_{2}
and
\mathrm{P}_{1},\mathrm{P_{2}}
are probabilistic
event structures.

3.

v(\emptyset)=1

It follows directly from the definition.

4.

d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0
for all
n\geq 1
and

y,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
with

y\subseteq x_{1},\dots,x_{n}

By
[
19
, Proposition 5]
we only need to check the condition for

y\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}\ x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
,
i.e.

y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{1},\dots,e_{n}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \ x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
. We have three
cases:

(a)

y=\emptyset

We then have
\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{\tau}
.

It follows that

d^{(1)}_{v}[\emptyset\,;\,\set{\tau}]=v(\emptyset)-v(\set{\tau})=1-1=0

(b)

y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})

We have three cases (let
1\leq i\leq n
):

i.

\forall e_{i}\in E_{1}

We have
x_{1}\backslash\tau,\dots,x_{n}\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
.
Let
I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n}
.

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i})

\displaystyle=p\cdot\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v_{1}((y\backslash\tau)\bigcup_{i\in I}(x_{i}\backslash\tau))

\displaystyle=p\cdot d^{(n)}_{v_{1}}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]

Since
p\in]0,1[
and
d^{(n)}_{v_{1}}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]
, because
\mathrm{P}_{1}
is a
probabilistic event structure, then
p\cdot d^{(n)}_{v_{1}}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0
.

ii.

\forall e_{i}\in E_{2}

Since
y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
,
x_{i}=\set{e_{i}}\cup y
, and for all

1\leq i\leq n
we have
e_{i}\#e\in y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
, then

v(x_{i})=0
. Hence

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i})

\displaystyle=v(y)

\displaystyle=p\cdot v_{1}(y\backslash\tau)

Since
p\in]0,1[
and
v_{1}(y\backslash\tau)\geq 0
, because
\mathrm{P}_{1}
is a
probabilistic event structure, we have
p\cdot v_{1}(y\backslash\tau)\geq 0
.

iii.

\exists e_{i}\in E_{2}

Since
y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
,
x_{i}=\set{e_{i}}\cup y
, and for all

1\leq i\leq n
such that
e_{i}\#e\in y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
, we have

v(x_{i})=0
. Hence

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{I^{\prime}}(-1)^{|I^{\prime}|}v(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I^{\prime}}x_{i})

\displaystyle=p\cdot\sum_{I^{\prime}}(-1)^{|I^{\prime}|}v_{1}((y\backslash\tau)\bigcup_{i\in I^{\prime}}(x_{i}\backslash\tau))

\displaystyle=p\cdot d^{(m)}_{v_{1}}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{m}]

where
I^{\prime}=\set{1,\dots,m}
,
i.e.

I^{\prime}
is
I
without those
e_{i}\in E_{2}
.

Since
p\in]0,1[
and
d^{(m)}_{v_{1}}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{m}]
, because
\mathrm{P}_{1}
is a
probabilistic event structure, then
p\cdot d^{(m)}_{v_{1}}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{m}]\geq 0
.

(c)

y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})

Similar to previous case.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.10

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

By Lemma
2.17
we know that
\mathrm{E}
is an event structure.
Hence we focus solely on the valuation part.

1.

v(\emptyset)=1

\displaystyle v(\emptyset)=v_{1}(\emptyset\cap E_{1})\cdot v_{2}(\emptyset\cap E_{2})=v_{1}(\emptyset)\cdot v_{2}(\emptyset)=1\cdot 1=1

2.

d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0
for all
n\geq 1
and

y,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
with

y\subseteq x_{1},\dots,x_{n}

By
[
19
, Proposition 5]
we only need to check the condition for

y\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}\ x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
,
i.e.

y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{1},\dots,e_{n}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \ x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
. We want to
show that

d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]=d^{(n_{1})}_{v_{1}}[y\cap E_{1}\,;\,x_{1}\cap E_{1},\dots,x_{n}\cap E_{1}]\cdot d^{(n_{2})}_{v_{2}}[y\cap E_{2}\,;\,x_{1}\cap E_{2},\dots,x_{n}\cap E_{2}]

Let
I_{1}\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n_{1}}
,
I_{2}\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n_{2}}
, and

I=I_{1}\uplus I_{2}
.

\displaystyle d^{(n_{1})}_{v_{1}}[y\cap E_{1}\,;\,x_{1}\cap E_{1},\dots,x_{n}\cap E_{1}]\cdot d^{(n_{2})}_{v_{2}}[y\cap E_{2}\,;\,x_{1}\cap E_{2},\dots,x_{n}\cap E_{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}}(-1)^{|I_{1}|}v_{1}\left((y\cap E_{1})\cup\left(\bigcup_{i\in I_{1}}x_{i}\cap E_{1}\right)\right)\cdot\sum_{I_{2}}(-1)^{|I_{2}|}v_{2}\left((y\cap E_{2})\cup\left(\bigcup_{j\in I_{2}}x_{j}\cap E_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}}(-1)^{|I_{1}|}v_{1}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I_{1}}x_{i}\right)\cap E_{1}\right)\cdot\sum_{I_{2}}(-1)^{|I_{2}|}v_{2}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{j\in I_{2}}x_{j}\right)\cap E_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}}\sum_{I_{2}}(-1)^{|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|}v_{1}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I_{1}}x_{i}\right)\cap E_{1}\right)v_{2}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{j\in I_{2}}x_{j}\right)\cap E_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1},I_{2}}(-1)^{|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|}v_{1}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I_{1}}x_{i}\right)\cap E_{1}\right)v_{2}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{j\in I_{2}}x_{j}\right)\cap E_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1},I_{2}}(-1)^{|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in(I_{1}\uplus I_{2})}x_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.14

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle P=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

\displaystyle P\backslash a=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime},\,v^{\prime})

By Lemma
2.24
we know that
\mathrm{E^{\prime}}
is an event structure.
Hence we focus solely on the valuation part.

1.

v^{\prime}(\emptyset)=1

\displaystyle v^{\prime}(\emptyset)=\dfrac{v_{1}(\emptyset\cup\set{a})}{v_{1}(\set{a})}=\dfrac{v_{1}(\set{a})}{v_{1}(\set{a})}=1

2.

d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0
for all
n\geq 1
and

y,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
with

y\subseteq x_{1},\dots,x_{n}

By
[
19
, Proposition 5]
we only need to check the condition for

y\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}\ x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
,
i.e.

y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{1},\dots,e_{n}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \ x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.

Let
I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,n}
.

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{v}[y\,;\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}\dfrac{v_{1}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)\cup\set{a}\right)}{v_{1}(\set{a})}\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\sum_{I}(-1)^{|I|}v_{1}\left(\left(y\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)\cup\set{a}\right)\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow d^{(n)}_{v_{1}}[y\cup\set{a}\,;\,x_{1}\cup\set{a},\dots,x_{n}\cup\set{a}]\geq 0

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.15

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1},\,v^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2},\,v^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime},\,v^{\prime})

Due to
Lemma
2.25
, we only need to show

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}),y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})\,.\,f[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
.

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
and
y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})
such
that
f[x]\subseteq y
. We have three cases:

1.

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that
x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
and

y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})
such that
y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1})

It follows directly that
v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)\Leftrightarrow v_{1}(x)\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y)
, since

v(x)=v^{\prime}_{1}(x)
,
v^{\prime}(y)=v^{\prime}_{1}(y)
and
\mathrm{P}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}
.

2.

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that
x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
and

y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})
such that

\exists y_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}),y_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})
such that

y=y_{1}\cup(y_{2}\times\{y_{1}\})
.

We know that
v(x)=v_{1}(x)
and that
v^{\prime}(y)=v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})
. Since

\mathrm{P}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}
then
f_{1}[x]\subseteq y_{1}
and
v_{1}(x)\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})
. It
then follows directly that

v(x)=v_{1}(x)\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})=v^{\prime}(y)
.

3.

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

\exists x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}),x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

x=x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})
and
y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})
such that

\exists y_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}),y_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})
such that

y=y_{1}\cup(y_{2}\times\{y_{1}\})

We know that
v(x)=v_{1}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})
and
v^{\prime}(y)=v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})
. Since

\mathrm{P}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}
then
f_{1}[x_{1}]\subseteq y_{1}
and

v_{1}(x_{1})\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})
, and
\mathrm{P}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}
then

f_{2}[x_{2}]\subseteq y_{2}
and
v_{2}(x_{2})\geq v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})
.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle v_{1}(x_{1})\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\leq v_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}\leq 1

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\left(\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}=1\right)\text{ or }\left(\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}<1\right)

Now we show that
v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
.

\displaystyle v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow v_{1}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow v_{2}(x_{2})\geq\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})

We have two cases:

1.

\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}=1

v_{2}(x_{2})\geq\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})\Leftrightarrow v_{2}(x_{2})\geq v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})
and we are done.

2.

\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}<1

Since
v_{2}(x_{2})\geq v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})
and
v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})\geq\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})

it follows that

v_{2}(x_{2})\geq v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})\geq\dfrac{v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})}{v_{1}(x_{1})}\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.16

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1},\,v^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2},\,v^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime},\,v^{\prime})

1.

We start by defining
f:E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
such that

f(e)=\begin{cases}e&\text{ if }e=\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\\
f_{1}(e)&\text{ if }e\in E_{1}\\
f_{2}(e)&\text{ if }e\in E_{2}\end{cases}

It is straightforward to see that
f
is injective.

2.

\pi(f(e))=\pi(e)

If
e\in E_{1}
or
e\in E_{2}
we are done, since
\mathrm{P}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{P}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}
.
If
e=\tau
, then
\pi(f(e))=\pi(e)
, and we are done.

3.

e\leq e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow f(e)\leq^{\prime}f(e^{\prime})

If
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{1}
or
e,e^{\prime}\in E_{2}
we are done, since
\mathrm{P}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathrm{P}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}
.
If
e=\tau
then
f(e)
. Consequently
e\leq e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e\leq^{\prime}f(e^{\prime})
, which is trivially satisfied by Definition
3.7
.

4.

e\#e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow f(e)\#^{\prime}f(e^{\prime})

Since
\tau
is not in conflict with any event, this case is similar to that of Lemma
2.26
.

5.

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2}),y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\,.\,f[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
and

y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,+_{p}\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that
f[x]\subseteq y
.
We have two cases:

(a)

x\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
and
y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1})

It follows directly that
v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
, since
\mathrm{P}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}
and

v(x)=p\cdot v_{1}(x\backslash\tau)\geq p\cdot v^{\prime}_{1}(y\backslash\tau)=v^{\prime}(y)
.

(b)

x\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
and
y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})

It follows directly that
v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
, since
\mathrm{P}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}
and

v(x)=(1-p)\cdot v_{2}(x\backslash\tau)\geq(1-p)\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y\backslash\tau)=v^{\prime}(y)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.17

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{1},\,v^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime}_{2},\,v^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime},\,v^{\prime})

Due to Lemma
2.27
, we only need to show

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2}),y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})\,.\,f[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
.

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
and
y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})

such that
f[x]\subseteq y
. Since
\mathrm{P}_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1}
then

\forall x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}),y_{1}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{1})
such that

f_{1}[x_{1}]\subseteq y_{1}
we have
v_{1}(x_{1})\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})
and that

\mathrm{P}_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2}
entails

\forall x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2}),y_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{2})
such that

f_{2}[x_{2}]\subseteq y_{2}
we have
v_{2}(x_{2})\geq v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})
. By
Definition
3.9
,
v(x)=v_{1}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})
and

v^{\prime}(y)=v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})
, where
x_{1}=x\cap E_{1}
,
x_{2}=x\cap E_{1}
,

y_{1}=y\cap E^{\prime}_{1}
, and
y_{2}=y\cap E^{\prime}_{2}
. We then have:

\displaystyle v(x)=v_{1}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})\geq v^{\prime}_{1}(y_{1})\cdot v^{\prime}_{2}(y_{2})=v^{\prime}(y)

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.18

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{1\,;\,2},\,v_{1\,;\,2})

\displaystyle(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l=(\mathrm{E}_{1}^{l},\,v_{1}^{l})

\displaystyle(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime},\,v^{\prime})

\displaystyle l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})

Due to Lemma
2.29
we only need to show

1.

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l),y\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\,.\,f[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l)
and

y\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

f[x]\subseteq y
. We have two cases:

(a)

\set{l}\cup x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

\set{l}\cup x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})

\displaystyle v(x)=\dfrac{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l}\cup x)}{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l})}=\dfrac{v_{1}(\set{l}\cup x)}{v_{1}(\set{l})}=v_{1}^{l}(x)=v^{\prime}(x)

Since
(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}
is a probabilistic event structure, then for

x,y\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

f[x]\subseteq y
, we have
v^{\prime}(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
, since

d^{(1)}_{v^{\prime}}[x\,;\,y]\geq 0\Leftrightarrow v^{\prime}(x)-v^{\prime}(y)\geq 0\Leftrightarrow v^{\prime}(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
.

Hence
v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
.

(b)

\set{l}\cup x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

\exists(\set{l}\cup x_{1})\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1}),x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
where

\set{l}\cup x=(\set{l}\cup x_{1})\cup(x_{2}\times\{\set{l}\cup x_{1}\})

\displaystyle v(x)=\dfrac{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l}\cup x)}{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l})}=\dfrac{v_{1}(\set{l}\cup x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})}{v_{1}(\set{l})}=v_{1}^{l}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})=v^{\prime}(x)

Since
(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}
is a probabilistic event structure, we obtain

v(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
.

2.

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}),y\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l)\,.\,f[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v^{\prime}(x)\geq v(y)

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
and

y\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l)
such that
f[x]\subseteq y
. We have
two cases:

(a)

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)

\displaystyle v^{\prime}(x)=v_{1}^{l}(x)=\dfrac{v_{1}(\set{l}\cup x)}{v_{1}(\set{l})}=\dfrac{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l}\cup x)}{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l})}=v(x)

Since
(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l
is a probabilistic event structure, we obtain

v^{\prime}(x)\geq v(y)
.

(b)

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

\exists x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l),x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
such that

x=x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})

\displaystyle v^{\prime}(x)=v_{1}^{l}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})=\dfrac{v_{1}(\set{l}\cup x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})}{v_{1}(\set{l})}=\dfrac{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l}\cup x)}{v_{1\,;\,2}(\set{l})}=v(x)

Since
(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l
is a probabilistic event structure, we obtain

v^{\prime}(x)\geq v(y)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.19

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},\,v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},\,v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E},\,v)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l=(\mathrm{E}^{\prime},\,v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l=(\mathrm{E}_{1}^{l},\,v_{1}^{l})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}\backslash l=(\mathrm{E}_{2}^{l},\,v_{2}^{l})

\displaystyle(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{l},\,v^{l})

\displaystyle l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})

Due to Lemma
2.29
, and similarly to it, we focus when
l\in\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{E}_{1})

and only show

1.

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l),y\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\,.\,f[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v^{\prime}(x)\geq v^{l}(y)

\displaystyle v^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{v(\set{l}\cup x)}{v(\set{l})}=\dfrac{v_{1}((\set{l}\cup x)\cap E_{1})\cdot v_{2}((\set{l}\cup x)\cap E_{2})}{v_{1}(\set{l})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{v_{1}(\set{l}\cup(x\cap E_{1}))\cdot v_{2}(x\cap E_{2})}{v_{1}(\set{l})}=v_{1}^{l}(x\cap E_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x\cap E_{2})=v^{l}(x)

Since
(\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2}
is a probabilistic event structure, we obtain

v^{\prime}(x)\geq v^{l}(y)
.

2.

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\backslash l)\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2}),y\in\mathcal{C}((\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l)\,.\,f[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v^{l}(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)

\displaystyle v^{l}(x)

\displaystyle=v_{1}^{l}(x\cap E_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x\cap E_{2})=\dfrac{v_{1}(\set{l}\cup(x\cap E_{1}))\cdot v_{2}(x\cap E_{2})}{v_{1}(\set{l})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{v_{1}((\set{l}\cup x)\cap E_{1})\cdot v_{2}((\set{l}\cup x)\cap E_{2})}{v_{1}(\set{l})}=\dfrac{v(\set{l}\cup x)}{v(\set{l})}=v^{\prime}(x)

Since
(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})\backslash l
is a probabilistic event structure, we obtain

v^{l}(x)\geq v^{\prime}(y)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.20

Proof.

It follows directly from Definition
3.9
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.21

Proof.

•

sk\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket skip\rrbracket)

It follows directly that
v(\set{sk})=1
.

•

a\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket a\rrbracket)

It follows directly that
v(\set{a})=1
.

•

\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

It follows directly that
v(\set{\tau})=1
.

•

l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

By Definition
3.5
we have
l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)
. By i.h.,

v(\set{l^{\prime}})=1
and since
l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket)
we are done.

•

l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

By Definition
3.9
we have
l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)
or

l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)
. By i.h.,
v(\set{l^{\prime}})=1
for both cases. Since

l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket)
we are done.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.22

Proof.

Follows directly from the respective definitions.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.23

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}

\displaystyle=(E,\,\leq,\,\#,\,v)

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{i}

\displaystyle=(E_{i},\,\leq_{i},\,\#_{i},\,v_{i})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}

\displaystyle=(E^{\prime}_{i},\,\leq^{\prime}_{i},\,\#^{\prime}_{i},\,v^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}

\displaystyle=(E^{1},\,\leq^{1},\,\#^{1},\,v^{1})

\displaystyle(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i})\,||\,\mathrm{P}

\displaystyle=(E^{2},\,\leq^{2},\,\#^{2},\,v^{2})

\displaystyle\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P})

\displaystyle=(E^{3},\,\leq^{3},\,\#^{3},\,v^{3})

1.

\left(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}\right)\,||\,\mathrm{P}\sqsubseteq\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P})

We know that
E^{2}=E^{1}\uplus E=(\{\tau\}\uplus\biguplus_{i}E_{i})\uplus E
and

E^{3}=\{\tau\}\uplus\biguplus_{i}(E_{i}\uplus E)
. Note that with

\biguplus_{i}(E_{i}\uplus E)
, for each
i
we are making a copy of
E
. We then denote the
events of
E
from
\biguplus_{i}(E_{i}\uplus E)
as
e^{i}
, for each
i
(it is important to
highlight that the plain events of
e
and
e^{i}
are the same) and we write
E^{(i)}
for the

P
-copy inside branch
i
.

(a)

We begin by defining, for a fixed
i
,
f_{i}:E^{2}\rightarrow E^{3}
such that

f_{i}(e)=\begin{cases}e&\text{ if }e\in\{\tau\}\uplus E_{i}\subseteq E^{1}\\
e^{i}&\text{ if }e\in E\end{cases}

Since
i
is fixed, it is straightforward to see that
f_{i}
is injective (
f_{i}
is essentially
the identity map, where for each
i
it creates a copy of the events in
E
).

(b)

\pi(f_{i}(e))=\pi(e)

If
e\in\{\tau\}\uplus E_{i}
then we are done since
f_{i}(e)=e
.
If
e\in E
, then
\pi(f_{i}(e))=\pi(e^{i})=\pi(e)
, since
e^{i}
is a copy of
e
for a fixed
i
.

(c)

e\leq^{2}e^{\prime}\implies f_{i}(e)\leq^{3}f_{i}(e^{\prime})

Assume
e\leq^{2}e^{\prime}
. By Definition
3.9
,
e\leq^{1}e^{\prime}
or
e\leq e^{\prime}
. If

e\leq e^{\prime}
, then by Definition
3.9
and for a given
i
,

f_{i}(e)\leq^{\prime}_{i}f_{i}(e^{\prime})
. By Remark
3

f_{i}(e)\leq^{3}f_{i}(e^{\prime})
.

If
e\leq^{1}e^{\prime}
, then by Remark
3
, exists
i
such that
e\leq_{i}e^{\prime}
. By
Definition
3.9
,
e\leq^{\prime}_{i}e^{\prime}
. By Remark
3

f_{i}(e)\leq^{3}f_{i}(e^{\prime})
.

(d)

e\#^{2}e^{\prime}\implies f_{i}(e)\#^{3}f_{i}(e^{\prime})

Similar to the previous case.

(e)

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}((\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i})\,||\,\mathrm{P}),y\in\mathcal{C}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}))\,.\,f_{i}[x]\subseteq y\Rightarrow v^{2}(x)\geq v^{3}(y)

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}((\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i})\,||\,\mathrm{P})
,

y\in\mathcal{C}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}))
, such that

f_{i}[x]\subseteq y
.

By Definition
3.9
and Remark
3
we have:

v^{2}(x)=\begin{cases}p_{i}v_{i}((x\cap E^{1})\backslash\tau)\cdot v(x\cap E)&\text{ if }(x\cap E^{1})\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{i})\\
v(x\cap E)&\text{ if }(x\cap E^{1})=\emptyset\text{ or }(x\cap E^{1}=\{\tau\})\end{cases}

By Remark
3
we have:

v^{3}(x)=\begin{cases}p_{i}v^{\prime}_{i}(y\backslash\tau)&\text{ if }y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P})\\
1&\text{ if }(y=\{\tau\})\text{ or }(y=\emptyset)\end{cases}

We have the following cases:

i.

y=\emptyset

Thus
v^{3}(y)=1
. Since
f_{i}[x]\subseteq y
, then
x=\emptyset
. Thus

v^{2}(x)=v(\emptyset\cap E)=v(\emptyset)=1
.

ii.

y=\{\tau\}

Thus
v^{3}(y)=1
. Since
f_{i}[x]\subseteq y
, then
x=\emptyset
or
x=\{\tau\}
. If

x=\emptyset
we are done. If
x=\{\tau\}
, then

v^{2}(\{\tau\})=v(\{\tau\}\backslash E)
, which entails
\{\tau\}\cap E^{1}=\{\tau\}
.
Then
\{\tau\}\cap E=\emptyset
since
\tau\not\in E
. Thus

v(\{\tau\}\cap E)=v(\emptyset)=1
.

iii.

y\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P})

Thus
v^{3}(y)=p_{i}\cdot v^{\prime}_{i}(y\backslash\tau)
. By Definition
3.9
,

v^{\prime}_{i}(y\backslash\tau)=v_{i}(y\backslash\tau\cap E_{i})\cdot v(y\backslash\tau\cap E^{(i)})
. Since
\tau\not\in E_{i}
and
\tau\not\in E
, then

y\backslash\tau\cap E_{i}=y\cap E_{i}
and

y\backslash\tau\cap E^{(i)}=y\cap E^{(i)}
. Thus

p_{i}\cdot v^{\prime}_{i}(y\backslash\tau)=p_{i}\cdot v_{i}(y\cap E_{i})\cdot v(y\cap E^{(i)})
.

Since
f_{i}[x]\subseteq y
, if
x=\emptyset
or
x=\{\tau\}
we know that
v^{2}(x)=1
.
Hence
1\geq p_{i}\cdot v_{i}(y\cap E_{i})\cdot v(y\cap E)
, since
v_{i}
and
v
are
configuration-valuations.

It lacks to consider the case where

v^{2}(x)=p_{i}v_{i}((x\cap E^{1})\backslash\tau)\cdot v(x\cap E)
. To show that

p_{i}v_{i}((x\cap E^{1})\backslash\tau)\cdot v(x\cap E)\geq p_{i}\cdot v_{i}(y\cap E_{i})\cdot v(y\cap E^{(i)})
, it suffices to show that

v_{i}((x\cap E^{1})\backslash\tau)\geq v_{i}(y\cap E_{i})
and

v(x\cap E)\geq v(y\cap E^{(i)})
, since product is monotone in each factor.

We argue first that
v(x\cap E)\geq v(y\cap E^{(i)})
. We know that

f_{i}[x_{i}\cap E]=\{f_{i}(e)\mid e\in x\cap E\}=f_{i}[x_{i}]\cap E^{(i)}
. This set is
composed by the events of
E
that belong in
x
in the
i
-th copy. Thus, we have

f_{i}[x_{i}\cap E]=f_{i}[x_{i}]\cap E^{(i)}\subseteq y\cap E^{(i)}
, from

f_{i}[x_{i}]\subseteq y
. Since
P
is a probabilistic event structure, then

v(f_{i}[x\cap E])\geq v(y\cap E^{(i)})
. Hence
v(f_{i}[x\cap E])=v(x\cap E)
. Thus

v(x\cap E)\geq v(y\cap E^{(i)})
. With similar arguments, we have

v_{i}((x\cap E^{1})\backslash\tau)\geq v_{i}(y\cap E_{i})
. Thus

p_{i}v_{i}((x\cap E^{1})\backslash\tau)\cdot v(x\cap E)\geq p_{i}\cdot v_{i}(y\cap E_{i})\cdot v(y\cap E^{(i)})
.

2.

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\left(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}\right)\,||\,\mathrm{P})
iff

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}))

For both cases, it is relevant to notice the following: let
\mathrm{P}_{1},\mathrm{P}_{2}
be two
probabilistic event structures such that
x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})
and

x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})
. Then
x=x_{1}\cup x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}\,||\,\mathrm{P}_{2})
,
since by Definition
3.9
there is no conflict between events of
\mathrm{P}_{1}
and
events of
\mathrm{P}_{2}
.

\Leftarrow

If
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\left(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}\right)\,||\,\mathrm{P})

then
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}))

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\left(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}\right)\,||\,\mathrm{P})
. We can
represent
x
as follows:
x=x\cap(E\uplus E_{i})=(x\cap E)\uplus(x\cap E_{i})
, for

E_{i}
in
\biguplus_{i}E_{i}
and where
x\cap E\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P})
and

x\cap E_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i})
.
Hence, it follows directly that

(x\cap E)\uplus(x\cap E_{i})=x\cap(E\uplus E_{i})=x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}))
.

\Rightarrow

If
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}))
then

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\left(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}\right)\,||\,\mathrm{P})

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P}))
. Hence

\exists x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}),y\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P})
such that

x=x_{i}\cup y
. Since
x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i})
, then

\exists i\,.\,x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{i})
. We then have

x_{i}\cup y=x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\mathrm{P}_{i}\,||\,\mathrm{P})
and consequently

x\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\left(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\mathrm{P}_{i}\right)\,||\,\mathrm{P})
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.24

Proof.

1.

C\equiv C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

We know that

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,;\,C_{2})
.
By the rules in Figure
13
we have

C_{1}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i})
. By i.h. we have

x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
and

x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
such that

\exists\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket_{\gamma}\,.\,v_{1}(x_{1})=v_{i}(x_{1})
. Let

x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
. By Definition
3.5
we have

x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
and

x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}((\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket_{\gamma})\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
, and

v(x)=v_{1}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})=v_{i}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})
. By Lemma
3.22

we have

x_{1}\cup(x_{2}\times\{x_{1}\})\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
.

2.

C\equiv C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}

We know that

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,||\,C_{2})
.
By the rules in Figure
13
we have

C_{1}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i})
. By i.h. we have

x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
and

x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
such that

\exists\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket_{\gamma}\,.\,v_{1}(x_{1})=v_{i}(x_{1})
. Let

x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
. By Definition
3.9
we have

x_{1}\cup x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
and

x_{1}\cup x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}((\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket_{\gamma})\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{\gamma})
, and

v(x)=v_{1}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})=v_{i}(x_{1})\cdot v_{2}(x_{2})
. By Lemma
3.23

we have
x_{1}\cup x_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket)_{\gamma}
.

A similar reasoning is applied when

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{j}p_{j}\cdot(\tau,C_{2}\,||\,C_{j})
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.25

Proof.

Induction over
C
.

•

C\equiv skip
.

It follows directly that
skip\twoheadrightarrow 1\cdot(sk,\checkmark)

•

C\equiv a
.

It follows directly that
a\twoheadrightarrow 1\cdot(a,\checkmark)

•

C\equiv C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}

By Figure
13
,

C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C_{1})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C_{2})
. By i.h.,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
,

\sum_{m}p_{m}(\omega_{m},C_{m})
s.t.

C_{1}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
and

C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{m}p_{m}(\omega_{m},C_{m})
. By Figure
14
,

C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\tau:\omega_{n},C_{n})+\sum_{m}p_{m}(\tau:\omega_{m},C_{m})
.

•

C\equiv C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

According to Figure
13
we have three cases:

1.

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{2})

By i.h.,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
s.t.

C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
. By
Figure
14
,

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(l:\omega_{n},C_{n})
.

2.

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{1}^{\prime}\,;\,C_{2})

By i.h.,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
s.t.

C_{1}^{\prime}\,;\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
. By
Figure
14
,

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(l:\omega_{n},C_{n})
.

3.

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,;\,C_{2})

By i.h.,
\forall i
,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{in},C_{in})
s.t.

C_{i}\,;\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{in},C_{in})
. By
Figure
14
,

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\sum_{n}p_{n}(\tau:\omega_{in},C_{in})
.

•

C\equiv C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}

According to Figure
13
we have three cases:

1.

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{2})

By i.h.,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
s.t.

C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
. By
Figure
14
,

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(l:\omega_{n},C_{n})
.

2.

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{1}^{\prime}\,||\,C_{2})

By i.h.,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
s.t.

C_{1}^{\prime}\,||\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
. By
Figure
14
,

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(l:\omega_{n},C_{n})
.

3.

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,||\,C_{2})

By i.h.,
\forall i
,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{in},C_{in})
s.t.

C_{i}\,||\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{in},C_{in})
. By
Figure
14
,

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\sum_{n}p_{n}(\tau:\omega_{in},C_{in})
.

•

C\equiv\mu X.D

According to Figure
13
we have two cases:

1.

\mu X.D\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,D^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\mu X.D])

By i.h.,
\exists

\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},D_{n})
s.t.

D^{\prime}[X\leftarrow\mu X.D]\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},D_{n})
.
By Figure
14
,

\mu X.D\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(l:\omega_{n},D_{n})
.

2.

\mu X.D\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}(\tau,D_{i}[X\leftarrow\mu X.D])

By i.h.,
\forall i\ \exists\ \sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{in},D_{in})
s.t.

D_{i}[X\leftarrow\mu X.D]\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{in},D_{in})
. By Figure
14
,

\mu X.D\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\sum_{n}p_{n}(\tau:\omega_{in},D_{in})
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.26

Proof.

Induction over rules in Figure
13
.

•

skip\rightarrow 1\cdot(sk,\checkmark)

It follows directly that
\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket skip\rrbracket\backslash sk\equiv\emptyset
.

•

a\rightarrow 1\cdot(a,\checkmark)

It follows directly that
\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket a\rrbracket\backslash a\equiv\emptyset
.

•

C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C_{1})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C_{2})

It follows directly that

p\cdot\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket=\llbracket C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rrbracket
.

•

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{2})

\displaystyle C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket,\text{ Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-rem-init2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2})

\displaystyle C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-rem-init2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,;\,C_{2})

\displaystyle C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,;\,C_{2})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:seq-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:sound-aux-seq-2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{2})

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init2}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,C_{2})

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,C_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init2}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,||\,C_{2})

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i}\,||\,C_{2})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{1}\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot(\tau,C_{i})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq(\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:sound-aux-conc-2}, Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\sum_{i}p_{i}\cdot\llbracket C_{i}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}\checkmark

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init2}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{2}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-rem-init2}},\text{Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l

•

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{j}p_{j}\cdot(\tau,C_{1}\,||\,C_{j})

\displaystyle C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{j}p_{j}\cdot(\tau,C_{1}\,||\,C_{j})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-small2} entails}}

\displaystyle C_{2}\rightarrow\sum_{j}p_{j}\cdot(\tau,C_{j})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{i.h.}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\sum_{j}p_{j}\cdot\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:conc-mono2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\sum_{j}p_{j}\cdot\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\set{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:sound-aux-conc-2}, Definition~\ref{def:den-sem2}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\sum_{j}p_{j}\cdot\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{j}\rrbracket

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.27

Proof.

Induction over the size of
\omega_{0}
.

•

|\omega_{0}|=1

We have that
C\twoheadrightarrow 1\cdot(l,\checkmark)
. It follows directly that

\{l\}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
such that
\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \{l\}
and

v(\{l\})=1
.

•

|\omega|>1

Let us rewrite
p_{0}(\omega_{0},\checkmark)+\sum_{k}p_{k}(\omega_{k},C_{k})
as

\sum_{ij}p_{ij}(\omega_{ij},C_{ij})

\displaystyle C\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{ij}p_{ij}(\omega_{ij},C_{ij})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\{\text{Figure~\ref{fig:op-nstep2} entails}\}

\displaystyle C\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}(l^{\prime},C_{i})\qquad\forall i\,C_{i}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{j}p_{j}(\omega_{ij}^{\prime},C_{ij})

We have two cases:

1.

Case
l^{\prime}\neq\tau

\displaystyle C\rightarrow 1\cdot(l^{\prime},C^{\prime})\qquad C^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow p_{0}(\omega_{0}^{\prime},\checkmark)+\sum_{j\neq 0}p_{j}(\omega_{j}^{\prime},C_{j})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\{\text{Lemma~\ref{res:soundI-2}, i.h.}\}

\displaystyle\llbracket C\rrbracket\backslash l^{\prime}\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\qquad\exists x_{0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket)\text{ such that }\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{j}^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ x_{0}^{\prime}\text{ and }p_{0}=v^{\prime}(x_{0}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\{\text{Definition~\ref{def:rem-init2}}\}

\displaystyle\{l^{\prime}\}\cup x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)\text{ such that }\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \{l^{\prime}\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{j}^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \{l^{\prime}\}\cup x_{0}^{\prime}\text{ and }p_{0}=v(\{l^{\prime}\}\cup x_{0}^{\prime})

2.

Case
l^{\prime}=\tau

\displaystyle C\rightarrow\sum_{i}p_{i}(l^{\prime},C_{i})\qquad\exists i\,C_{i}\twoheadrightarrow p_{0}^{\prime}(\omega_{i0}^{\prime},\checkmark)+\sum_{j\neq 0}p_{j}(\omega_{ij}^{\prime},C_{ij})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\{\text{Lemma~\ref{res:soundI-2}, i.h.}\}

\displaystyle\llbracket C\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\sum_{i}p_{i}\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\exists i,\exists x_{i0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket)\text{ such that }\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{i0}^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ x_{i0}\text{ and }p^{\prime}_{0}=v_{i}(x_{i0})

Now we have two sub-cases:

(a)

Case
C=C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}

By Definition
3.13
,

\exists i,\exists\set{\tau}\cup x_{i0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
such that

\displaystyle\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{\tau}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{i0}^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{\tau}\cup x_{i0}^{\prime},\ v_{i}(\set{\tau}\cup x_{i0}^{\prime})=p_{i}\cdot p_{0}^{\prime}=p_{0}

(b)

Case
C=C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}
or
C=C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}

By Remark
3
and Lemma
3.24

\exists i,\exists\set{\tau}\cup x_{i0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
such that

\displaystyle\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{\tau}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{i0}^{\prime}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \set{\tau}\cup x_{i0}^{\prime},\ v_{i}(\set{\tau}\cup x_{i0}^{\prime})=p_{i}\cdot p_{0}^{\prime}=p_{0}

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.28

Proof.

•

sk\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket skip\rrbracket)

Let
C^{\prime}=\checkmark
. It follows directly that
skip\rightarrow 1\cdot(sk,\checkmark)

and that
\llbracket skip\rrbracket\backslash sk\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
.

•

a\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket a\rrbracket)

Let
C^{\prime}=\checkmark
. It follows directly that
a\rightarrow 1\cdot(a,\checkmark)

and that
\llbracket a\rrbracket\backslash a\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
.

•

\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

By Definition
3.7
we have that

\llbracket C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rrbracket=p\cdot\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
,
hence
\tau\in\mathcal{I}(p\cdot\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)
. Let

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)
By Definition
3.7
and Lemma
3.21
we
have that
v(\set{\tau,l})=p\cdot v_{1}(\set{l})=p
. It then follows directly that

C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C_{1})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C_{2})
and

\llbracket C_{1}\,+_{p}\,C_{2}\rrbracket=p\cdot\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.
Similarly we do the same when
l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)
.

•

l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

We have two cases:

1.

l^{\prime}\neq\tau

By Definition
3.5
we have that
l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)
. By i.h.,

\exists C^{\prime}
such that
C_{1}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime})
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket
. We have two cases:

(a)

C^{\prime}=\checkmark

We have
C_{1}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,\checkmark)
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure
13
,

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{2})
.
By Definition
3.5
,
(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket=\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.

(b)

C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{1}

We have
C_{1}\xrightarrow{l}C^{\prime}_{1}
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket
. By the rules in Figure
13
,

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2})
. By
Definition
3.5
,

(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.
By Definition
3.11
,
\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket
.

2.

l^{\prime}=\tau

We have
\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket)
, which by
Definition
3.5
gives us that
\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)
. By i.h.,

\exists C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}
such that

C_{1}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime\prime})
with

p=v(\set{\tau,e^{\prime}})
and
e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(C^{\prime})
, and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket
. By the rules
in Figure
13
, we have

C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime}\,;\,C_{2})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime\prime}\,;\,C_{2})
.
By Lemma
3.15
,

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,;\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq(p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)\,;\,C_{2}
.
By Lemma
3.22
and Definition
3.11
,

\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket
.

•

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket)

We have two cases:

1.

l^{\prime}\neq\tau

By Definition
3.9
we have two cases:

(a)

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)

By i.h.
\exists C^{\prime}\,.\,C_{1}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime})
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
By the rules in Figure
13
we have two cases:

i.

C^{\prime}=\checkmark

We have
C_{1}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,\checkmark)
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
. By the rules in
Figure
4
we have

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{2})
. By
Definition
3.9
,
(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.
By Lemma
3.19
we have

(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
.
By Definition
3.11
,
\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

ii.

C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{1}

We have
C_{1}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime}_{1})
and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{1}\rrbracket
. By the rules in
Figure
13
we have

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,C_{2})
. By
Definition
3.9
,
(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\backslash l)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.
By Lemma
3.19
we have

(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
. By Definition
3.11
,

\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

(b)

l\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)

By i.h.
\exists C^{\prime}\,.\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime})
and

\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket
. By the rules in
Figure
13
we have two cases:

i.

C^{\prime}=\checkmark

We have
C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,\checkmark)
and

\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket\checkmark\rrbracket
. By the rules in
Figure
13
we have

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{1})
. By
Definition
3.9
,
\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)
.
By Lemma
3.19
we have

(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
.
By Definition
3.11
,
\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

ii.

C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{2}

We have
C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C^{\prime}_{2})
and

\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}_{2}\rrbracket
. By the rules in
Figure
13
we have

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow 1\cdot(l,C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}_{2})
. By
Definition
3.9
,
\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l)
.
By Lemma
3.19
we have

(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)\backslash l
. By Definition
3.11
,

\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\backslash l
.

2.

l^{\prime}=\tau

We have
\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket)
, which by
Definition
3.9
entails
\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)
or

\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)
.
We have two cases:

(a)

\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket)

By i.h.,
\exists C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}
such that

C_{1}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime\prime})
with

p=v(\set{\tau,e^{\prime}})
and
e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket)
, and

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket
. By the rules
in Figure
13
, we have

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime}\,||\,C_{2})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime\prime}\,||\,C_{2})
.
By Lemma
3.17
,

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq(p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
.
By Lemma
3.23
and Definition
3.11
,

\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket
.

(b)

\tau\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket)

By i.h.,
\exists C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}
such that

C_{2}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime\prime})
with

p=v(\set{\tau,e^{\prime}})
and
e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket)
, and

\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket
. By the rules
in Figure
13
, we have

C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime\prime})
.
By Lemma
3.17
,

\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\,||\,\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq C_{1}\,||\,(p\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)
.
By Lemma
3.23
and Definition
3.11
,

\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq p\cdot\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\cdot\llbracket C_{1}\,||\,C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.29

Proof.

Induction over the size of
\omega_{x_{0}}
.

•

|\omega_{x_{0}}|=1

We have
\{l\}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
. It follows directly that

C\twoheadrightarrow 1\cdot(l,\checkmark)
and
v(\{l\})=1
.

•

|\omega_{x_{0}}|>1

We have
x_{0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
.
We know that
\omega_{x_{0}}=l_{0}l_{1}\dots l_{n}
.
Hence
\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l_{0}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \{l_{0}\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{x_{0}^{\prime}}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ \{l_{0}\}\cup x_{0}^{\prime}
.
We then have
l_{0}\in\mathcal{I}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)
.
By Lemma
3.28
we have two cases:

1.

l_{0}\neq\tau

Hence
C\rightarrow 1\cdot(l_{0},C^{\prime})
and

\llbracket C\rrbracket\backslash l_{0}\equiv\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket
. By Definition
3.13
,

x_{0}\backslash l_{0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket)
such that

\emptyset\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{x_{0}^{\prime}}}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ x_{0}\backslash l_{0}
. By i.h.,

C^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow v(x_{0}\backslash l_{0})(\omega_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\checkmark)+\sum_{k}p_{k}(\omega_{k},C_{k})
, for some
p_{k},\omega_{k},C_{k}
. By Figure
14
,

C\twoheadrightarrow v(x_{0}\backslash l_{0})(l_{0}:\omega_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\checkmark)+\sum_{k}p_{k}(l_{0}:\omega_{k},C_{k})
, for some
p_{k},\omega_{k},C_{k}
.

2.

l_{0}=\tau

Hence
C\rightarrow p\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime})+(1-p)\cdot(\tau,C^{\prime\prime})
and

\llbracket C\rrbracket\sqsubseteq p\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket
.
Now we have two sub-cases:

(a)

Case
C=C^{\prime}\,+_{p}\,C^{\prime\prime}

We then have
\llbracket C\rrbracket=p\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket
, by
Definition
3.11
, and consequently

x_{0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(p\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)
. By
Definition
3.7
,
x_{0}\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket)
or

x_{0}\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)
. We only consider the former, since the
latter has a similar reasoning. By i.h.,

C^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow v(x_{1})(w_{x_{1}},\checkmark)+\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
, for some
p_{n},\omega_{n},C_{n}
. By Lemma
3.25
,

C^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{m}p_{m}(\omega_{m},C_{m})
.
By Figure
14
,

\displaystyle C\twoheadrightarrow

\displaystyle p\cdot\left(v(x_{1})(\tau:w_{x_{1}},\checkmark)+\sum_{n}p_{n}(\tau:\omega_{n},C_{n})\right)

\displaystyle+(1-p)\cdot\sum_{m}p_{m}(\tau:\omega_{m},C_{m})

(b)

Case
C=C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}
or
C=C_{1}\,||\,C_{2}

By Lemma
3.24
,

x_{0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(p\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket+(1-p)\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)
. By
Definition
3.7
,
x_{0}\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket)
or

x_{0}\backslash\tau\in\mathcal{C}_{\text{max}}(\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket)
. We only consider the former, since the
latter has a similar reasoning. By i.h.,

C^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow v(x_{1})(w_{x_{1}},\checkmark)+\sum_{n}p_{n}(\omega_{n},C_{n})
, for some
p_{n},\omega_{n},C_{n}
. By Lemma
3.25
,

C^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\sum_{m}p_{m}(\omega_{m},C_{m})
.
By Figure
14
,

\displaystyle C\twoheadrightarrow

\displaystyle p\cdot\left(v(x_{1})(\tau:w_{x_{1}},\checkmark)+\sum_{n}p_{n}(\tau:\omega_{n},C_{n})\right)

\displaystyle+(1-p)\cdot\sum_{m}p_{m}(\tau:\omega_{m},C_{m})

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.33

Proof.

Due to Lemma
2.40
we only need to check the condition of the valuations. Consider

\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},v_{1})
,
\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},v_{2})
, and
\mathrm{P}_{3}=(\mathrm{E}_{3},v_{3})
to be
probabilistic event structures.

•

Reflexivity:
\mathrm{P}_{1}=\mathrm{P}_{1}

We want to show that
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})\,.\,v_{1}(x)=v_{1}(x)
.
It holds straightforwardly.

•

Transitivity:
\mathrm{P}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{2},\ \mathrm{P}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{3}\Rightarrow\mathrm{P}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{3}

We want to show
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})\,.\,v_{1}(x)=v_{3}(x)
.
From
\mathrm{P}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{2}
,
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})\,.\,v_{1}(x)=v_{2}(x)
.
From
\mathrm{P}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{3}
,
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})\,.\,v_{2}(x)=v_{3}(x)
.
Hence,
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})\,.\,v_{1}(x)=v_{3}(x)
.

•

Antisymmetry:
\mathrm{P}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{2},\ \mathrm{P}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{1}\Rightarrow\mathrm{P}_{1}=\mathrm{P}_{2}

We want to show
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}),\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})\,.\,v_{1}(x)=v_{2}(x)
.
From
\mathrm{P}_{1}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{2}
,
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1})\,.\,v_{1}(x)=v_{2}(x)
.
From
\mathrm{P}_{2}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{1}
,
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})\,.\,v_{2}(x)=v_{1}(x)
.
Hence,
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{1}),\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{2})\,.\,v_{1}(x)=v_{2}(x)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.34

Proof.

We first show that
\bot
is a probabilistic event structure. From Lemma
2.41

\bot=(\emptyset,\,\emptyset,\,\emptyset)
is an event structure. It lacks to see the
conditions on the valuations. It follows directly the definition that
v(\emptyset)=1
. Furthermore the only configuration in
\mathcal{C}(\bot)
is
\emptyset
. Hence we trivially have
that
v(\emptyset)\geq 0
.

To show that
\bot
is the least element, consider any probabilistic event structure
\mathrm{P}
. We
need to show that
\bot\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}
. Due to Lemma
2.41
we focus
solely on the valuations. Since the empty configuration is the only one in
\mathcal{C}(\emptyset)

and since
\mathrm{P}
is a probabilistic event structure it holds that

v_{\bot}(\emptyset)=1=v(\emptyset)
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.36

Proof.

Due to Lemma
2.43
we focus only on the valuation part, where we have two conditions to verify:

•

v^{\omega}(\emptyset)=1

From Definition
3.35
we know that

\exists n\in\omega\,.\,v^{\omega}(\emptyset)=v_{n}(\emptyset)=1
.

•

\forall y,x_{1},\dots,x_{m}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\omega})
such that

y\subseteq x_{1},\dots,x_{m}
,

\sum_{I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,m}}(-1)^{|I|}v^{\omega}(y\cup\cup_{i\in I}x_{i})\geq 0

Following
[
19
, Propostion 5]
we only need to focus on
y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.25006pt\hbox{$\rule{11.38092pt}{0.5pt}$}\cr\hbox{$\subset\mkern-12.0mu$}\cr}}}}}\ x_{1},\dots,x_{m}
.
From Definition
3.35
we know it
\exists n\in\omega\,.\,v^{\omega}(y)=v_{n}(y)
. We
then have three cases, depending if the events are in
E_{n}
, in
E_{n+1}
, or in both.

1.

the events are in
E_{n}

We know that

\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,m}}(-1)^{|I+1|}v^{\omega}(\cup_{i\in I}x_{i})=\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,m}}(-1)^{|I+1|}v_{n}(\cup_{i\in I}x_{i})

and consequently

v_{n}(y)-\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,m}}(-1)^{|I+1|}v_{n}(\cup_{i\in I}x_{i})\geq 0
, since
\mathrm{P}_{n}
is a probabilistic event structure

2.

the events are in
E_{n+1}

We know that
v_{n}(y)=v_{n+1}(y)
since
\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{n+1}
. Furthermore

\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,m}}(-1)^{|I+1|}v^{\omega}(\cup_{i\in I}x_{i})=\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,m}}(-1)^{|I+1|}v_{n+1}(\cup_{i\in I}x_{i})

and consequently

v_{n+1}(y)-\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\set{1,\dots,m}}(-1)^{|I+1|}v_{n+1}(\cup_{i\in I}x_{i})\geq 0

since
\mathrm{P}_{n+1}
is a probabilistic event structure

3.

the events are in both

Since
\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}_{n+1}
we know that

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{n})\,.\,v_{n}(x)=v_{n+1}(x)
, which leads us to the previous
case.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.37

Proof.

Due to Lemma
2.44
we focus only on the valuations.

•

\mathrm{P}^{\omega}
is an upper bound

\forall n\in\omega
we need to have
\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}^{\omega}
. It follows
directly from Definition
3.32
that
\forall n\in\omega
we have

\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}^{\omega}
since by Definition
3.35
,

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\omega}),\,\exists n\in\omega\,.\,v^{\omega}(x)=v_{n}(x)
.

•

\mathrm{P}^{\omega}
is the least upper bound

Let
\mathrm{P}=(\mathrm{E},v)
be an upper bound of the chain. We need to show that if

\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}^{\omega}
and
\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}
then

\mathrm{P}^{\omega}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}
. From
\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}^{\omega}
,

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{n})\,.\,v_{n}(x)=v^{\omega}(x)
. From Definition
3.35
,

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\omega}),\ \exists n\in\omega\,.\,v^{\omega}(x)=v_{n}(x)
. From

\mathrm{P}_{n}\trianglelefteq\mathrm{P}
,
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}_{n})\,.\,v_{n}(x)=v(x)
. Thus

\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}^{\omega}),\exists n\in\omega\,.\,v^{\omega}(x)=v_{n}(x)=v(x)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.38

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}=(\mathrm{E},v)

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}_{1},v_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}_{2},v_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{1}=(\mathrm{E}^{1},v^{1})

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{2}=(\mathrm{E}^{2},v^{2})

We want to show
\forall x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{1})\,.\,v^{1}(x)=v^{2}(x)
. Due to
Lemma
2.46
we only focus on the valuations.
According to Definition
3.5
we have two cases:

1.

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{1})
such that
x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P})

Then we are done because
v^{1}(x)=v(x)=v^{2}(x)
.

2.

x\in\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{P}\,;\,\mathrm{P}_{1})
such that
